# CLAIM LEDGER — every asserted-but-unproven invariant, and what proves it

The complete inventory behind `docs/TESTING.md` §2. A **claim** is a
statement the code asserts (doc comment, safety posture, honest-UI promise) without a
programmatic proof. This file is the project's test metric: **claims proven / claims
provable** — never line coverage. Update the verdict column when a proof lands, with the
proof's name; add a row when a new invariant is written into a comment.

Compiled 28 Jul 2026 from a full sweep of `src-tauri/src` (~1,037 invariant-language
comment lines read against 535 lib tests and 105 examples). Line numbers are anchors,
not contracts — trust the file, verify the line.

**Maintenance rule (also in CLAUDE.md): an invariant comment isn't finished until its
ledger row exists with a verdict — same commit, like the TODO tick.** A ledger that
drifts is worse than none; this project has already shipped one false safety comment
and two doc-drift audits. **The rule covers the TALLY as much as the row** — that half
was skipped once and is now enforced by `scripts/ledger-tally.sh --check` in
`verify.sh`, so a row can no longer land while the summary still describes the file
as it was yesterday.

**Standing first step for any row whose subject is a THIRD PARTY'S behaviour**
(⚠ rows, engine/runtime/tool internals): before designing a test, MEASURE which
layer can even observe the subject. Twice in one week that measurement killed a
planned test that would have passed forever — #2/#33's live CF-header run replaced
years of simulated headers, and #40/#166's queued L2 legs died on the fact that
Playwright contains neither subject (`docs/archive/PLAN-webview-dialog-proofs.md`). A
verdict layer assigned without that measurement is a guess wearing a plan's
clothes.

Verdicts:
- ✅ **proven** — a named lib test or example exercises exactly this claim.
- ◐ **half-proven** — the stated half is proven; the noted half is not (layer in note).
- 🔨 **provable-unproven** — no proof exists; the layer that could settle it is named.
  These rows ARE the backlog.
- 🚫 **inherently unprovable** — needs a real second device / third-party behavior /
  human eye. Each maps to a scripted-manual item (PLAN §5) or is an accepted posture.

Layers (PLAN §1): L0 = lib test · L1 = example vs real binary · L2 = WebKit harness ·
L3 = scripted manual.

⚠ = security posture resting on an assumption about a third party's behavior — the
`wp_tunnel` "a dead tunnel receives no requests" shape that burned us.

## core/wp_tunnel.rs

| # | Anchor | Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | wp_tunnel.rs:8 | Quick-tunnel URL invisible to PHP, can't be read from the request | 🔨 L1 (assert cloudflared forwards no public-host header) |
| 2 | wp_tunnel.rs:8 | ⚠ CF-header set is a sound AND complete tunnel discriminator | ◐ **MEASURED live 14 Aug 2026, through a quick tunnel** (`tunnel_exposure_check` legs 1–2), which is the first time this premise was checked at all: every other guard in the tree SIMULATES the headers in PHP. Through the tunnel: `CF-Ray=a2aabcfa3f232508-DAC`, `CF-Connecting-IP=103.209.197.170`. The same endpoint locally: neither header, so the discriminator is complete as well as sound. **Scoped like #228's row, because this is a fact about CLOUDFLARE and not about our code**: it is what was observed, on that date, through a `trycloudflare.com` quick tunnel. If Cloudflare stops sending the set, rexenv's posture does not fail loudly — the URL rewriter silently stops activating and the login denial silently stops firing. **This leg is a WATCH, not a settlement**, and leg 1's failure message says so to whoever trips it |
| 3 | wp_tunnel.rs:19 | Local requests untouched while shared | ✅ `tunnel_muplugin_check` mode 1 |
| 4 | wp_tunnel.rs:22 | mu-plugin lifetime ≤ tunnel + one relaunch | ◐ sweep fates proven (`tunnel_sweep`); the bound across a real crash+relaunch 🔨 L1 |
| 5 | wp_tunnel.rs:87 | Host regex never rewrites lookalike domains | ✅ `tunnel_muplugin_check` buffer mode |
| 6 | wp_tunnel.rs:134 | Content dir read from record, never derived at write time | ✅ `bedrock_layout_writes_where_wp_loads…` |
| 7 | wp_tunnel.rs:139 | Origin validation: nothing escapes the PHP string | ✅ `validate_origin_rejects_php_string_escapes` |
| 8 | wp_tunnel.rs:189 | Removing from a layout that never had them changes nothing | ✅ same test as #6 |
| 9 | wp_tunnel.rs:32 | Subdomain multisite shares main site only; subdirectory whole-network | ◐ **the subdirectory half was FALSE until 26 Aug 2026** — written as a scope note, never checked. The `option_siteurl`/`option_home` filters returned the BARE public origin, so a sub-site lost its `/sub1`: its front page 404'd, and a login at `/sub1/wp-login.php` landed on the MAIN site's dashboard (measured live through a quick tunnel, real browser, subdirectory network). Fixed by swapping the origin and KEEPING the path; `tunnel_muplugin_check` now asserts a sub-site's siteurl/home keep the blog path, under the REAL bundled PHP. Still ◐, not ✅: the probe proves the FILTERS, and the live subdirectory run is one dated observation, not a gate. **The subdomain half was MEASURED 27 Aug 2026 and is TRUE — but incomplete as written**: through a real tunnel the network's sites list renders the sub-site as `s1.msd.rex`, a host no public visitor resolves and the one pinned `--http-host-header` can never carry, and the rewrite's boundary correctly leaves it alone. What the note implies and does NOT hold is that the main site is therefore usable: on a subdomain install WP pins `COOKIE_DOMAIN` to `.<network domain>` in `ms_cookie_constants()`, which runs before mu-plugins, so every auth cookie is dropped by the browser and login answers "Cookies are blocked". **Fixed 27 Aug 2026** by the wp-config cookie-scope block (#415–#417): live re-run of the same flow gives host-only cookies, 302 → `/wp-admin/`, **"Dashboard ‹ MSD"** in curl AND in real Chrome, with the local network unchanged in the same run |
| 415 | wp_tunnel.rs (cookie-scope template) | wp-config is the ONLY seat: `COOKIE_DOMAIN` is pinned in `ms_cookie_constants()` before mu-plugins load | ✅ `tunnel_muplugin_check` asserts the block closes BEFORE the `wp-settings.php` require, matching on the require line and not the first mention — the block's own comment names the file, so the lazy match would have passed wherever it landed. Live 27 Aug 2026: subdomain login through a real tunnel, 302 → "Dashboard ‹ MSD", curl and Chrome |
| 416 | wp_tunnel.rs (cookie-scope template) | ⚠ Fires ONLY for a CF-marked request WITH a live rexenv share — the second condition is what keeps it out of production | ✅ all four combinations run under the REAL bundled PHP (`tunnel_muplugin_check` leg 4): CF+share → `COOKIE_DOMAIN=[]`, CF-without-share and both local cases → undefined. The second condition is not belt-and-braces: on the header alone, this file copied to a Cloudflare-fronted production network would empty the constant there and break cross-subdomain SSO — a failure that reads as "users get logged out on subsites" with nothing pointing at rexenv |
| 417 | wp_tunnel.rs `ensure_subdomain_cookie_scope` | Written once, never removed — and a block missing a delimiter never reaches disk | ✅ `cookie_scope_lands_before_wp_settings_and_only_once` (second call leaves the file byte-identical) + `cookie_scope_skips_every_config_that_does_not_need_it` (subdirectory network, single site, and no wp-config at all stay untouched) + `…refuses_loudly_rather_than_sharing_a_network_nobody_can_log_into` (no anchor → the START fails rather than handing out a URL that refuses every login; an unrecorded content dir is never interpolated into PHP). **Permanence is the safe direction**: a leftover block is inert, while a half-applied removal from wp-config takes the whole site down |
| 418 | wp_tunnel.rs (siteurl/home host scope) | Only a value on the PINNED host is rewritten — another blog's host is left alone | ✅ `tunnel_muplugin_check` leg 2 asserts `http://s1.mysite.test/` and the lookalike come back unchanged while the pinned host's value is rewritten path-and-all. **Found by using it, not by reading it** (27 Aug 2026): on a subdomain network's My Sites, "MSD" and "Sub Domain One" both read Visit → `<origin>/` and Dashboard → `<origin>/wp-admin/` — a link that silently lands on the wrong site. Rewriting cannot make a sub-site reachable (one tunnel, one Host), so the only thing it can do is lie. Live after the fix: the sub-site reads `https://s1.msd.rex/` and an outside Chrome gets `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` |
| 419 | wp_tunnel.rs `SUNRISE_TEMPLATE` | sunrise is the last seat where a Host rewrite still counts, and `$wpdb` is already live there | ✅ read off the shipped core, not assumed: `wp-settings.php` calls `require_wp_db()` (136) then requires `ms-settings.php` (161), which includes the drop-in (52) BEFORE reading `HTTP_HOST`/`REQUEST_URI` to resolve the blog (62–77). `tunnel_muplugin_check` leg 4 runs the drop-in under the REAL bundled PHP with a `$wpdb` stub: `/s1/wp-admin/` → `HOST=s1.mysite.test`, an unowned label and `/wp-content/…` are left alone, and a request with no CF headers is inert |
| 420 | wp_tunnel.rs `SUNRISE_TEMPLATE` | ⚠ Only the Host moves — REQUEST_URI keeps its prefix | ✅ asserted for `/s1/wp-admin/`, `/s1` and `/s1/?p=7`. **This is the counter-intuitive half and it was learned the hard way** (27 Aug 2026): stripping the prefix looked obviously right and sent an unauthenticated `/s1/wp-admin/` to the sub-site's login with `redirect_to=<origin>/wp-admin/` — the MAIN site's dashboard. `WP::parse_request()` already strips `home_url()`'s path, and `home_url()` is `<origin>/s1` by then, so the prefix costs routing nothing and everything WP builds from `REQUEST_URI` needs it |
| 421 | services.rs `rewrite_block` | Subdomain vhosts carry the network rewrite rules; inert locally, load-bearing through a tunnel | ✅ `rewrite_slots_differ_by_mode` asserts the rules AND the `!-e $request_filename` guard are present for both network modes. Locally every sub-site's `/wp-admin/` exists on disk so the guard never opens — re-measured live in the same run: `s1.msd.rex/` 200 native, `msd.rex/s1/` **404** (locally it is NOT a subdirectory network), cookie still `domain=.msd.rex`. Through the tunnel `/s1/wp-admin/` has no file behind it and the rules point nginx at the real one, while `REQUEST_URI` stays `$request_uri` — the ORIGINAL — which is what sunrise reads. Take either half away and the sub-site admin 404s |
| 422 | wp_tunnel.rs `enable`/`disable` | The two halves share ONE lifetime | ✅ `tunnel_muplugin_check` leg 6: `disable` removes the mu-plugin AND the sunrise drop-in. A sunrise left behind would keep remapping requests with no rewriter to match; a rewriter left behind would link to hosts nobody can reach — both worse than neither |

## core/tunnels.rs + commands/tunnels.rs

| # | Anchor | Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | tunnels.rs:8 | ⚠ Sharing one site can't expose another site or a tool | ◐ live 14 Aug 2026 (`tunnel_exposure_check` leg 6): through a real tunnel the shared site's page is served, and the SAME request with `Host: other.test` does not return the other site's page. **Stated honestly: this is a WATCH on cloudflared, not a guard on our code.** Removing the `--http-host-header` pin does not fail this leg — it fails the FIXTURE, because the tunnel then serves nothing at our host (404). So the plant proves the pin is load-bearing without proving the leg can catch a caller-Host override; that property belongs to cloudflared and would change only if cloudflared changed |
| 11 | tunnels.rs:66 | api.trycloudflare.com never recorded as public URL | ✅ `extract_url_never_takes_the_registration_endpoint` |
| 12 | tunnels.rs (`PID_PENDING`, `may_signal`) | **The pending sentinel can never authorise a signal.** A row claims its pid BEFORE the binary resolves and spawns, so a crash in that window leaves a row naming `u32::MAX` and no process that ever existed — any argv the process table answers for it belongs to a stranger. `u32::MAX` being unassignable is inertness on today's platform, not a rule, so the refusal is now explicit and happens BEFORE the table is consulted at all | ✅ L0 `the_pending_sentinel_can_never_authorise_a_signal` (2 Sep 2026) — the sentinel is refused even when handed a command line that satisfies the FULL identity, which is what a recycled `u32::MAX` would have to look like; plus `every_sweep_signal_is_authorised_by_the_predicate`, a drift guard holding both sweeps to the one predicate (`stop`, the user pressing Stop with the live `Child`'s own pid, is the named exception). **Plant-proven ×2**: deleting the sentinel arm, and routing a sweep round the predicate |
| 13 | tunnels.rs:110 (the refusal) + core/sites.rs `is_nginx_served` + examples/override_fallthrough_check | Override-site refusal; premise: default-server fallthrough publishes another site | ✅ **premise CONFIRMED live 14 Aug 2026.** An override-server site gets NO nginx server block (`rebuild_configs_for` filters on `is_nginx_served`, sites.rs:1999), so a request carrying its Host falls through to the first vhost: `Host: fpsite.test` was served **`first.test`'s** bytes. Controls in the same run — each nginx site serves its OWN marker — so "another site came back" is distinguishable from "this nginx serves one thing to every Host", and the check refuses with CONTROLS BROKEN if they fail. **So the refusal is protecting against something real.** **No tunnel is used, deliberately:** a tunnel's whole contribution here is `--http-host-header`, i.e. a Host header on a loopback request to nginx, and #10's leg 3 already measured live that the Host arriving at nginx through a real tunnel is the site's own domain. The premise is those two halves composed — re-standing a public tunnel would re-prove the finished half while adding a public URL to a question that has nothing to do with Cloudflare. Structural half asserted BEFORE serving: if an override site ever gains an nginx vhost the check fails loudly and says the refusal needs re-reading rather than the check re-running. **The refusal itself was RETIRED 15 Aug 2026** (per-backend origins, #332): an override site now shares from its OWN recorded backend port, so the fallthrough this row measured is no longer reachable from a tunnel start — the premise stays proven and is WHY `origin_port` may never answer nginx for an override site |
| 14 | tunnels.rs:137 | Transport errors are non-evidence, never downgrade | ✅ `fold_probe_reads_one_fact_honestly` |
| 15 | tunnels.rs:150 | Any non-530 response proves the tunnel path | ◐ fold proven; semantic premise 🚫 |
| 16 | tunnels.rs (`diagnose_unreachable`) | **The edge address comes from a LIVE lookup, never a constant.** The failure-gated edge check reads the address out of 1.1.1.1's own answer for the share hostname, falling back to a live `trycloudflare.com` apex lookup. A pinned Cloudflare IP rots silently — the check keeps connecting to an address that stopped being an edge, and "the edge answered" then measures nothing while reading in the UI as positive evidence that the tunnel is registered | ✅ L0 `no_edge_ip_is_ever_hardcoded` (2 Sep 2026) — a source guard over the module's production lines: any dotted quad outside the three DELIBERATE addresses (the loopback origin, the `0.0.0.0` bind for our own ephemeral socket, and 1.1.1.1 — the resolver we query on purpose, which is the measurement rather than a pinned answer) fails by name. **Plant-proven**: replacing the apex fallback with a literal edge IP |
| 17 | tunnels.rs:282 | Passing edge check ≠ every POP routes; second device is the only e2e test | 🚫 → PLAN §5 |
| 18 | tunnels.rs:334 | 300s gate cap outlives any early negative-cache | 🚫 third-party resolver behavior |
| 19 | tunnels.rs:352 | Phase A never produces a system-DNS query | ◐ decision layer ✅ `phase_a_never_plans_a_system_dns_query`; reqwest `.resolve()` never falls back 🔨 L3 (dtrace procedure) |
| 20 | tunnels.rs (`PhaseGate`, `probe_plan`) | **The phase gate only ever opens — a share never re-enters Phase A.** Phase A exists so we make no system-resolver query for a name that may not be in public DNS yet: ONE early query negative-caches the LAN for up to thirty minutes (`trycloudflare.com` SOA MINIMUM 1800s, measured 28 Jul 2026 — it is why a phone reached a share only on cellular). A gate that reopened would push a WORKING share back into a window whose whole purpose is protecting one that does not resolve yet. It is now a TYPE with no closing transition rather than a `bool` and a rule: `open()` is the only mutation, `Default` is the closed state | ✅ L0 `the_phase_gate_only_ever_opens` (2 Sep 2026) — behavioural (a fresh share starts closed; repeated `open()` never comes back closed) AND on the type surface (exactly one write to the flag, and never to `false`), because a behavioural test alone passes on a type that has grown a `close()` nobody has called yet. **Plant-proven ×2**: adding `close()`, and defaulting the gate to open (which also reddens `phase_a_never_plans_a_system_dns_query`) |
| 21 | tunnels.rs:380 | Positive edge status never upgrades toward Reachable | ✅ `fold_diagnosis_reads_the_vector_honestly` +1 |
| 22 | tunnels.rs:395 | Token-match identity; recycled pid gets cleanup, never a signal | ✅ 2 lib tests + `tunnel_sweep` live |
| 23 | tunnels.rs (`spawn_args`, the ONE argv builder) | **We never spawn cloudflared without the `--http-host-header <domain>` pair — and what we spawn is what the sweeps can identify.** The pair is not only routing: it is the identity every ownership decision reads (`is_our_tunnel` before any signal, `host_header_domain` for the rowless backstop, the parent-death guard's re-read). A spawn missing it leaves a LIVE public share that every sweep declines to touch — unkillable by us and invisible to the ownership rules, for a process that publishes the developer's machine to the internet. The argv now has one builder, which is what makes the claim testable as a round trip rather than as a literal appearing in a function body | ✅ L0 `what_we_spawn_is_what_the_sweeps_can_identify` (2 Sep 2026) — the argv `start` actually spawns is fed to the identity functions the sweeps use and must be recognised by all three (`is_our_tunnel`, `host_header_domain`, `may_signal`), must NOT authorise a signal for a different row's domain, and carries a loopback origin on the port asked for. **Plant-proven ×2**: dropping the pair, and ignoring `origin_port` |
| 24 | commands/tunnels.rs (`start_tunnel` → `tunnel_site`) | **A tunnel target can only ever be a SITE row.** Adminer is a passwordless database browser on its own tooling vhost; the reason it can never be published is not a validation somebody has to get right, it is that `start_tunnel` resolves its target through a Sites lookup that errors on a miss, and a tooling vhost has no site row | ✅ L0 `a_tunnel_target_can_only_ever_be_a_site_row` — asserts the entry path still goes through `tunnel_site(&state, &id)`, that `start_tunnel` never names the Adminer host, and that `tunnel_site` still ERRORS rather than falling back (a fallback is how a non-site becomes a tunnel origin). **Verdict corrected 2 Sep 2026**: the row read 🔨 "negative test on the entry path" while that test existed — the row was stale, not the code |
| 25 | commands/tunnels.rs:56 | Deleted/renamed site never stays publicly reachable | ◐ live 14 Aug 2026 (`tunnel_exposure_check` leg 7) — and the leg took two attempts to become real. Its first version stopped the tunnel AND the stack, so "the public URL stopped answering" was indistinguishable from "nginx went away", and it PASSED with `tunnels::stop` neutered to a no-op. It now stops the tunnel only, PROVES the origin still serves afterwards, then requires the public URL to stop — **plant-proven** with the same no-op, which now fails naming the exposure. Delete ordering closed 15 Aug 2026 (#190, `tunnel_delete_order_check`); rename ordering rides the same `stop_for_domain` call at the top of `change_site_domain` |
| 26 | commands/tunnels.rs:102 | Start's claim never outlives stop/delete/rename | ◐ L0 ✅ `a_starts_claim_never_outlives_the_stop_that_revoked_it` — against real SQLite, not asserted about code: two concurrent starts and exactly one claim; the stop REVOKES by deleting the row (the fact an in-flight start with no registry entry reads); the domain is claimable again afterwards, or a stop would strand it; and `set_tunnel_pid` returns false on a revoked claim, which is how a start mid-spawn learns to cancel itself. **Plant-proven** by making `delete_tunnel` a no-op. Live leg — a real start racing a real stop — rides the one tunnel example (group A) |
| 27 | commands/tunnels.rs:117 | `Err` from try_wait reads alive; death needs positive evidence | ✅ `take_dead_removes_only_exited_children` |
| 28 | commands/tunnels.rs (the registry) + routes/Tunnels.tsx | **A tunnel that is not in the registry is not Live on screen either.** The registry half was proven: a dead child is settled out before the snapshot (#32). The screen half is that a site with NO tunnel row renders IDLE — the share button, not a badge — because the card reads the status reply rather than remembering what it painted last time | ✅ registry (#32's order guard) **+ ✅ L2 `scripts/wk-checks/tunnelhealth.js`'s idle leg (2 Sep 2026)**: the mock now carries a fourth site with no tunnel at all, and it must be marked not-live and say nothing about Live. That site is the CONTROL for the whole probe — without it, "the reachable card says Live" is a claim about a screen where every card happened to be a running share. **Plant-proven**: giving the un-shared site a tunnel fails both its assertions by name |
| 29 | commands/tunnels.rs:156 | rexenv never stops a share on the user's behalf | ◐ L0 ✅ the call-site half of `a_dead_child_never_reads_as_sharing_and_the_reaper_never_stops_a_share`. **The guard is aimed where the invariant became load-bearing, not where it was written**: the SCRATCH REAPER deletes sites unattended, and `delete_site_owned`'s first act is to stop the site's tunnel — so a reaper that took the normal path would break this rule with nobody watching. `commands/scratch.rs` must gate on `sharing_domain` and must never reach `stop_for_domain`; **plant-proven** by swapping the gate for a stop. (The reaper was written respecting this and cites #29 by number — the guard keeps that true rather than discovering it.) Live leg = #215's skip-don't-stop, same fixture, group A |
| 30 | commands/tunnels.rs:420 | Reaped pids never re-signalled in the exit hook | ◐ L0 ✅ `the_exit_hook_never_signals_a_reaped_or_sentinel_pid` — the decision is now a value (`should_signal_row`) rather than a condition inside a loop that needs a Tauri app to reach. Both refusals are the never-kill-on-a-bare-pid rule: a pid the registry pass already `wait()`ed is free for the OS to reuse, and `PID_PENDING` is a claim with no process behind it. An unreaped child from this session must still die, because tunnels die with the app. **Plant-proven** by dropping the reaped-set check. `tunnel_sweep` still covers launch only |
| 31 | commands/tunnels.rs:498 | A dead child never reads "already sharing" | ◐ L0 ✅ the ordering half of `a_dead_child_never_reads_as_sharing_and_the_reaper_never_stops_a_share`: BOTH readers (`sharing_domain`, `start_tunnel`) settle dead children before they read, so a crashed cloudflared can never answer "already sharing" — which would leave the user unable to re-share the site OR to run a job on it, since the step-7 guard reads the same fact. Source-ordering, because no value can carry it; **plant-proven** by moving the registry read above `take_dead()` |
| 430 | core/tunnels.rs `share_started_line` · commands/tunnels.rs | Every public share leaves an identifying line in `rexenv.log` — start AND stop | ◐ L0 ✅ two tests, both plant-proven. `a_share_line_names_the_site_the_url_and_the_pid` pins the CONTENT (site, public URL, pid — the three facts that turn "something is public" into "this process, this site, this link"), and `every_start_and_every_stop_writes_a_line` pins the CALL SITES, which no value can carry: start (after the registry insert, so a start that fails later never claims an exposure that never existed), user stop, the in-flight kill, the quit hook, and the crash path's existing WARN. Plants: dropping the start line, the stop line, or the quit line each fails. **Why the row exists**: on 27 Aug 2026 a share for `mstest.rex` was found running that nobody remembered starting, and `rexenv.log` had NO line for it — the whole record lived in `logs/tunnel-mstest.rex.log`, a file you can only think to open once you already know which domain to suspect, which is the question being asked. The evidence there (quick tunnel requested 14:12:18Z, its URL, traffic to `/sub1/…`) would have identified it in seconds from the app log. 🔨 the live leg — a real share's line read back out of a real `rexenv.log` — rides the one tunnel example (group A) |
| 431 | commands/system.rs (`StartupNotices`) + lib.rs launch sweeps + App.tsx (`StartupNoticeHost`) | **A share rexenv stops on the user's behalf is TOLD — once.** | ◐ L0 ✅ `startup_notices_survive_until_read_and_are_read_once` — the queue survives until something reads it, and a second read comes back EMPTY; plant-proven by making `drain` clone instead of take. **The bug this closes is a timing one, not a missing message**: the sweeps run inside `setup()`, before the webview exists, so an emitted event had nobody to reach — the WARN in `rexenv.log` was the whole surface, and a user whose public link went dead had no way to learn why except by reading a log. Draining is the other half: a notice that re-appears on every reload reads as a fault that keeps happening. 🔨 the live leg — a real crash-orphan sweep raising a real toast — rides the tunnel example |
| 432 | core/tunnels.rs (`guard_argv`, `parse_guard_args`, `GUARD_FLAG`) + platform/macos/parent_death_guard.rs + traits `guard_child_against_our_death` | **A public share dies with the app even when the app is SIGKILLed — and the guard never signals a pid it cannot still identify.** | ◐ L0 ✅ `a_guard_is_started_with_exactly_what_it_parses_back` (the argv is a contract between two processes: round-trip, plus eight malformed shapes that must REFUSE rather than default — a defaulted pid is a watcher waiting on init and a killer aiming at it) · L1 ✅ `tunnel_parent_death_check`, three legs against real processes and a real kernel: a SIGKILLed parent ends the share, a child whose argv names ANOTHER domain survives, and a child that dies first reaps the guard. **Plant-proven both directions** — never signalling fails leg 1, killing without `still_ours` fails leg 2. **Both plants first came back ALL PASS**, because `cargo run --example` does not rebuild the app BIN the guard runs from, so the example was agreeing with a change it had never executed; it now REFUSES when the binary predates the guard's source. **What it closes**: the crash window was bounded by nothing but how long the user took to relaunch — the rowless sweep's own row records shares that had been public for WEEKS. 🚫 the kqueue behaviour itself is the kernel's, not ours **Two hygiene defects** (post-0.4.0 review, 3 Sep 2026): the guard's `Child` was dropped at spawn, and `Child::drop` neither kills nor waits — one zombie per share, for the life of the app (a parked thread now reaps it); and `stop_child` shelled out to `kill` by PATH lookup two lines above a working `libc::kill` — in the one situation the guard exists for, a PATH that did not resolve `kill` left the share public, silently. `libc::kill` now. **The registration gap is CLOSED** (3 Sep 2026): the parent's start time (`ps -o lstart=`, `process_start_token`) rides argv as `GuardArgs::parent_start`, and the pid is checked against it BEFORE registering and AGAIN after — a pid that died and came back between the two wears a different start time, and either mismatch is read as "the parent is gone". The old four-element argv is now a malformed invocation (refused, never defaulted). Live: `tunnel_parent_death_check` leg 4 hands a guard a LIVE stand-in parent with a start time that is not its own, and the share must end while the stand-in survives — the recycled-pid shape without recycling a pid. |

A duration added at read time makes the promise "seven days from whenever something next looked" — a different sentence, and one nobody was shown. The backend reads the reason from wp-cli's OWN line (`Warning: Destination folder already exists. "…/plugins/<dir>/"`, measured verbatim 19 Aug 2026) as it streams past, and puts it on the job STATE (`blocked_by`); the card names that folder and offers a replace that re-runs the same job with `--force`. **One parser, on the state, because there are two readers and only one of them ever sees the log:** the first shipped version parsed the log in the card, and the TOAST — which receives only the state — went on announcing *"Install of thinkrank.1.31.0.zip failed: Error: No plugins installed."*, wp-cli's sentence and a lie about what happened, beside a card offering a one-click way forward. The toast now names the plugin and points at Replace, and the card's glyph/colour follow the same fact (amber `!`, not red `✕`) while wp-cli's summary line stays verbatim — re-toned, never paraphrased. The folder comes from that line rather than from the zip's filename, because a zip is routinely `plugin.1.2.3.zip` for a folder called `plugin`. **`force` defaults OFF at every layer** — the IPC param, the wrapper's default, and the CLI's explicit `false` (where `rex wp plugin install` takes wp.org slugs, and an existing install is an UPDATE question rather than an overwrite one) — and the retry keeps the job's own SOURCE, since re-sending a slug down the zip gate would fail `ensure_zip_paths` and read as a second, unrelated error. **One guard wp-admin has no need for:** rexenv knows which plugin directories are git checkouts because it put some of them there, and `--force` unpacks over the working tree — uncommitted changes, the branch, `.git` itself. The fixture's log lines are wp-cli 2.12.0's verbatim output against a real site, so the parser cannot pass on text wp-cli never prints. 🔨 → `wp_install_stream_check` gained jobs 5 and 6 (network tier): the SAME zip re-uploaded over the plugin it just installed must be refused **in the exact words the card parses** — the marker line, its quotes, and the folder name inside them — and the same job with `force` must then succeed with the directory still there. That leg is what turns wp-cli's wording from an assumption into a checked fact: if it changes, the Replace offer silently stops appearing and nothing else in the tree notices. **Ran green 19 Aug 2026** (stack stopped): `job5 (zip, already installed): status=failed` with the marker, its quotes and the folder name all present, then `job6 (zip, --force): status=ok` with the directory still in place. Measured on a real 47-plugin site (18 Aug 2026), the gate is a CAPABILITY check — `if ( ! current_user_can( 'manage_options' ) && ! $doing_cron ) { return; }` guarding the `new Updater(…)` that adds the `pre_set_site_transient_update_plugins` filter — and a wp-cli run has **no user at all**, so WordPress builds its update data with every paid plugin missing. Two grants, both measured rather than assumed: defining `WP_ADMIN` alone moved NOTHING (that experiment ran first), the three named capabilities moved four of five, and `WP_ADMIN` on top moved the fifth; 2 of 10 paid plugins reported an update before, 5 after, and the rest are ones wp-admin says nothing about either. The grant is queued with `WP_CLI::add_wp_hook` because the gates run on `init` — wp-cli's own `after_wp_load` is too late and `add_filter` does not exist yet at `after_wp_config_load`, both measured. **Nobody is impersonated:** no user is logged in, no session or cookie is created, the capabilities are NAMED one by one rather than blanket-granted, and the grant dies with the child. **The UPDATE path carries it too, and that is a correctness requirement, not symmetry:** the premium package URL comes out of the same filter, so `wp plugin update <paid-slug>` without the context answers "No plugin updates available" — measured — which would make the new badge a button that cannot work. **Where it does NOT ride is the claim with teeth**: not the fast list, not install/activate/deactivate/delete, not `core/terminal.rs`, and above all not `wp_run_raw`, the MCP raw runner an agent drives. Two failures fall out of that. **(a)** Under a runtime selection the sweep deletes, at the next launch, the tree the user just chose and is serving from; the 8 Aug plan promised "the new tree is not deleted" while the mechanism that would delete it went unmentioned — the plan and the code disagreed and neither knew. **(b)** It is already reachable WITHOUT any manifest: `restart_pools_for` propagates with `?` (service_manager.rs:1216-1219), so a failure on the first minor aborts the loop and later minors keep serving from their old masters — and lib.rs catches the error, logs, and falls through to the sweep, which unlinks exactly those trees. On macOS the process survives on the unlinked inode, so it looks healthy right up to the next restart. Same family as "one-fact lifetime guards": a check written against a fact (`patch == pin`) that holds today only because `seed_registry` writes it, kept pointing at the pin after the fact became mutable. The keep-set is now `{compiled-in pins} ∪ {each minor's REGISTERED patch}`, read from the registry rather than assumed. **Both halves are load-bearing** — keeping the pins is what makes "no network → falls back to the pins" an offline fallback rather than an offline download, since a floor whose bytes were deleted is not a floor | ✅ `the_gc_keeps_the_selected_patch_and_collects_the_pin_it_replaced` + `the_keep_set_is_deduplicated` (L0, `core::binaries`). **Plant-proven 16 Aug 2026**: restoring the pin-keyed rule (`match patch_for_minor { Some(pinned) => version != pinned, .. }`, ignoring the keep-set) turns the new test RED at the registered-tree assert while `outdated_php_cache_rule_is_narrow` stays GREEN — which is the point of shipping both, since the old test cannot see the defect and its continued green is the evidence that today's behaviour is unchanged (registered == pin for every row, so the two keep-sets are equal). The fixture is a patch of a REAL pinned minor (`<default minor>.9999`) so it takes the same branch a runtime selection would — prefix-stripped, strict `x.y.z`, minor has a pin — rather than a shape the narrow rules reject for unrelated reasons. The sweep is also now SKIPPED, loudly, when the registry can't be read: an empty keep-set is indistinguishable from "no minor runs anything", and deleting nothing is always safe. **Not covered:** that the sweep's caller passes the registry honestly is a lib.rs call-site fact with no L0 over it, and the unlink-a-live-master path (b) is L3 — it needs a running pool and a forced `restart_pools_for` failure **AMENDED 16 Aug 2026:** the keep-set's second half is no longer "each minor's registered patch" but **what the live masters are executing** (`running_patches`, #342) — the registry column it read was deleted by migration v36. The change is in the same direction as the original fix and for the same reason: the sweep deletes what is not in its keep-set, so that set must be a fact about the running processes rather than about anything mirroring a pin. `php.rs::registered_patches` is gone **AMENDED AGAIN 18 Aug 2026 — the pins half is GONE, and it was the expensive half.** "Both halves are load-bearing" above was wrong about the first one. `updates::floored` returns the pin ONLY when the pin is what the minor will run, and in that case the pin already IS that minor's effective patch — so the "keep the floor's bytes" clause protected nothing it was not already protecting, while alongside a higher selection it kept ~180 MB per updated minor forever. A user who updated 8.2 and 8.3 found 358 MB of trees nothing resolves, in a `bin/` at 3.3 GB, and reported it. The revert does not need them either: `php_update_apply` restores the previous SELECTION, which was effective at the last sweep and is therefore on disk; only a first-ever update reverts to the pin, and then the pin is still effective. What it trades away is a hypothetical "go back to the version this app ships" control that would re-download — there is no such control. **The half that replaced it is a refusal**, and it had to be: an unreadable registry arrives as an EMPTY effective set (`lib.rs` uses `unwrap_or_default`), which the pins used to mask; without them an empty keep-set means *delete every PHP tree on the machine*. `php_caches_to_keep` now returns `Option` and the caller skips. See #358 |
| 337 | src/styles/tokens.css (`--rex-text-label` and its light-theme comment, `--rex-text-dim`, `--rex-text-faint`) + every consumer, densest in shell/StatusFooter.tsx | **"mono section labels (darker: small type needs AA)"** — the light-theme `--rex-text-label` comment, which asserts WCAG AA for SMALL TYPE specifically. It is the only contrast claim anywhere in the codebase: `docs/DESIGN.md` states no contrast rule at all, so the standard is unstated and this lone comment is what a reader would take as the house position. **Measured 16 Aug 2026 and the claim is FALSE beyond its one surface.** `--rex-text-label` light reaches 4.70:1 on `--rex-surface-1` (pure white) and FAILS AA-normal on every other light surface it can sit on — bg 4.34, surface-2 4.16, surface-2-hover 3.90, well 3.90, surface-3 3.83. The DARK `--rex-text-label` carries no such comment and runs 2.14–2.78:1. Two neighbours fail everywhere in BOTH themes: `--rex-text-dim` (3.24–4.20 dark, 3.38–4.14 light) across **120 usages**, and `--rex-text-faint` (2.58–3.35 dark, 2.58–3.17 light) across 7. `--rex-text-muted` is the nearest passing token (4.66–6.04 dark). Sizes make it worse rather than better: StatusFooter renders these at 0.59375–0.65625rem, i.e. **9.5–10.5px**, which is squarely AA-normal's 4.5:1 threshold and not the 3:1 large-text one. **Same family as the four false guards already here ("guard covers claimed surface"):** a claim about small type generally, true at exactly one place inside the surface it claims. Found by an outside reader building the docs site, who muted the tokens in the site's replica and recorded WHY the replica diverges — the divergence note is the artifact worth keeping, since without it the next person files "the site does not match the app" | ✅ **CLOSED 16 Aug 2026 — refuted, then repaired.** The measurement was the proof and it came back negative; the fix followed. **The scan landed 16 Aug 2026** (`every_text_on_surface_pairing_meets_wcag_aa` in `core::copy_scan`), deriving both sets from the frontend's own `text-rex-*` / `bg-rex-*` usage so a token joins by being used rather than by being listed. It sized the debt at **39 failing pairs of 130 computed** — `text-dim` 13, `text-faint` 13, `text-label` 12, plus `accent-blue` on light `surface-3` at 4.49:1. Shipped as a **RATCHET rather than a red bar**: a red `verify.sh` blocks every commit through the pre-commit receipt, so the 39 are recorded in `KNOWN_DEBT` and the failing set must equal it EXACTLY — a new failure is red, and so is a pairing that starts PASSING, which forces progress to be recorded instead of quietly absorbed. Plant-proven three ways. **Exemptions were written from call sites, never from token names**: three `rgba` fills (they composite over the surface beneath, checked directly), and `accent-teal` as icon-only (1.4.11 non-text is 3:1, it clears 4.27). `accent-blue` was deliberately left IN despite being an icon colour too, because it is also a 13.5px tab label — exempting by name would have covered the label. **And the scan convicted something it was not looking for:** 17 Tailwind classes name a token that does not exist, so Tailwind emits no rule and the element inherits — `text-rex-text-secondary` x16 in `DbImportCard.tsx` (16 of its 18 text classes, so every 'secondary' line renders at full body brightness) and `bg-rex-surface-0` x1. Proven from the BUILT css, not from reading the config. **That half is CLOSED 16 Aug 2026**: `text-secondary` -> `text-muted` and `surface-0` -> `well` (what the other three log `<pre>` blocks already use), each replacement's contrast computed before it was written, and `KNOWN_DEBT` unmoved because `text-muted` was already in the used set. The `KNOWN_UNDEFINED` ratchet lived exactly one commit — it forced the repayment to be recorded, then was deleted with the debt, leaving the unconditional assertion. That lifecycle is the argument for a ratchet over an exemption. **And it is.** The contrast half closed the same day: the scan's own data said the three tokens were TEXT tokens, not ornament — 135 of 142 usages sat on `<div>/<span>/<li>` (emails, versions, paths, a pill label, placeholders), only 7 on icons. So the consumers moved to `text-muted` (AA-clean everywhere), `text-faint` and `text-label` went unused and were DELETED — which the sibling guard now polices, since a deleted token names nothing — `text-dim` survives on its 7 lucide glyphs, and light `accent-blue` was darkened one step (`#2e6f94` -> `#2e6e93`) for a pair that read 4.49:1, one hundredth under. **The scan also found the worst instance itself, in a place it could not originally see:** `globals.css` styled EVERY input's `::placeholder` with `--rex-text-faint` in raw CSS, 2.58-3.35:1 — the most widespread text in the app. The scan now reads `color: var(--rex-*)` from `src/styles` too. **And the exemptions were rebuilt after a plant walked through them.** They were declared BY TOKEN NAME (`text-dim` is icon-only), so moving `text-rex-text-dim` onto a `<span>` kept the exemption and the guard stayed green — the guard-covers-claimed-surface defect, committed inside the very guard written to end that family. Icons are now excluded STRUCTURALLY, at the call site, by reading which element the class sits on; the name-based entries are gone. Re-planted after: the same plant now fails with 13 pairs. **The ratchet's whole lifecycle is the method worth keeping:** 39 pairs recorded so the gate could be live while the fix was scoped, then a forced failure at '38 now PASS' that made the repayment be written down, then deletion along with the debt. Both debt lists are gone; both assertions are unconditional Deliberately NOT fixed on the 0.2.0 release branch: `tokens.css` is compiled into the artefact, the tagged dmg `bd019d8d…` was already built and A0-passed, and which way to resolve it is a design decision rather than a defect repair — promote consumers to `--rex-text-muted`, lighten the three tokens, or darken the surfaces, across 143 usages. **The layer that settles it permanently is L0 and it does not exist yet**: a derived scan over `tokens.css` computing every text-token x surface-token pair's ratio per theme and failing under 4.5:1, with an explicit allow-list for pairings that are genuinely decorative or never composed. That is the `core::copy_scan` shape — the comment stops being prose and becomes a check — and it is the reason this row is worth more than the three token edits. A scan also answers the question a hand-audit cannot: which pairs are actually COMPOSED in the app, versus merely possible. **And the arithmetic already constrains the fix:** lightening each token until it clears 4.5:1 against the worst surface it can sit on lands `text-dim`, `text-faint` and `text-label` ALL on approximately `--rex-text-muted` (dark ~#888e97, light ~#636974). At 9.5-10.5px a four-level text hierarchy and AA cannot both hold, so retuning the values would produce four tokens that look identical and a hierarchy that is dead in the design but alive in the code. The decision is therefore not which colours but which of the 143 usages are text somebody must READ versus ornament, which is exempt. Step-by-step plan in `docs/TODO.md`. **Extended 19 Aug 2026 (#373):** the scan reads two more routes into the palette — `text-status-*` and inline `style={{ color: var(--rex-*) }}` — and a sibling guard bans raw Tailwind hues outright. Both extensions convicted real light-mode text on the day they landed, which is the answer to whether the original scan's coverage was the whole surface: it was not |

| 307 | core/wp_login.rs (the client-IP gate) + examples/wp_login_client_ip_check + tunnel_exposure_check leg 8 | ⚠ **Cloudflare APPENDS to `X-Forwarded-For`; it does not replace it — so the leftmost read that shipped until 14 Aug 2026 was satisfiable by the caller.** Measured through a real quick tunnel 14 Aug 2026 (`tunnel_exposure_check`): an unspoofed request arrives as `X-Forwarded-For: 103.209.197.170` with `REMOTE_ADDR: 127.0.0.1` (cloudflared → nginx is local); a request that SETS the header arrives as `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1,103.209.197.170`. The gate took `trim(explode(',', $xff)[0])`, so the attacker's entry was the one read. **FIXED 14 Aug 2026 — the gate reads the LAST hop**, the entry written by the last proxy in the chain (our own edge locally, Cloudflare through a tunnel), never one the caller can prepend. **Scope CORRECTED 14 Aug 2026 by measuring the edge — an earlier version of this row claimed a LAN exposure that does not exist.** The reasoning was that the edge binds every interface, so a LAN caller could send `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1` and be read as loopback. Leg (E) of `wp_login_check` measured it instead of reasoning about it: a request through our edge carrying `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.7` arrives at PHP as exactly `127.0.0.1` — Caddy REPLACES a caller-supplied header with its own peer rather than appending to it (a Caddy default for untrusted proxies, so version-sensitive; the leg re-measures it rather than assuming). So on every path that traverses the edge, the caller's entry never reaches PHP, and a LAN caller was denied by their own real address before AND after this fix. **#307 was reachable through a tunnel and ONLY through a tunnel** — the tunnel is the one path that skips the edge (cloudflared → nginx direct), which is exactly why caller entries survive there. The inference was stated confidently for half a day; the measurement is what settled it. **What the fix buys, ruled 14 Aug 2026:** tunnel-replay protection now rests on TWO Cloudflare behaviours — that CF sends its header set, and that CF appends the connecting IP — where it rested on one; both must change before a captured link is replayable. **That is not two independent layers.** The Host gate is a second expression of the FIRST fact (it denies only because the tunnel mu-plugin restored the public host, gated on the same headers — #308), so nothing here is independent of Cloudflare. It raises the cost of the failure without changing its shape. **The mark that WOULD be independent, recorded with the objection attached so whoever picks it up starts from the objection rather than rediscovering it:** point cloudflared at its OWN loopback port and have nginx stamp an unforgeable `fastcgi_param` on that listener only — a header cannot choose a listen port. NOT done, and this is why: a tunnel adopted from an older running version arrives on the old port UNMARKED, so the new gate fails open across exactly one upgrade, and fail-open on an upgrade is the worst shape available. Any attempt must handle the adopted-tunnel case FIRST | ✅ parse fixed and proven at the layer it broke — `wp_login_client_ip_check` (sandbox tier) extracts the SHIPPED gate between its markers and runs it through real PHP over 13 cases, the tunnel shapes verbatim from the live measurement above. The matrix defends ITSELF against the three degenerate reads instead of relying on someone re-running plants: an allow-case and a deny-case share a leftmost entry (so a first-hop read cannot produce the table) and share a `REMOTE_ADDR` (so an XFF-ignoring read cannot either), both asserted before PHP starts. Plant-proved 3× anyway — restoring `[0]` fails 3 rows, deny-everything fails all 7 allows, allow-everything fails all 6 denies. ✓ The edge's own behaviour is now OBSERVED, not inferred: leg (E) of `wp_login_check` (run 14 Aug 2026) sends a caller `X-Forwarded-For` through the edge and asserts PHP sees exactly `127.0.0.1`, with a CONTROL header alongside it so "the edge dropped our header" cannot be confused with "the client never sent one" — the two readings are identical without it. Leg (F) is scoped in its own comment as indifference, NOT as proof the read moved: it would have passed before the fix too, because the edge discards the caller's entry either way. ✓ **END-TO-END PROVEN 14 Aug 2026 through four real quick tunnels** (`tunnel_exposure_check` leg 8): a magic link issued on the shared site and replayed at the public `*.trycloudflare.com` URL is denied — and the SAME token then logs in locally, so "denied" cannot be read as "already spent". The control reuses the token deliberately: `wp_login` rejects a tunnel attempt BEFORE consuming the option, so one artifact carries both halves. **The plant is three-part because nothing smaller can fail:** CF gate removed → still denied (the Host gate takes over); CF gate + tunnel Host-rewriter removed → **still denied**; all three removed → the replay LOGS IN. **So post-#307 the client-IP gate is INDEPENDENTLY SUFFICIENT against a real tunnel replay** — new as of this morning, because the leftmost read put Cloudflare's appended IP behind whatever a caller prepended, so the gate contributed nothing to this path before the fix. It rests on Cloudflare **appending the connecting IP to `X-Forwarded-For`** (fact 2), a DIFFERENT Cloudflare property from Cloudflare **sending its header set** (fact 1) — only fact 1 is what #2 watches. **What the leg still cannot see:** which gate fires on shipped code. That needed markers compiled into the mu-plugin and was answered once by hand (14 Aug 2026: `cf` fires first, the Host gate is never reached); the leg points at that observation rather than implying it re-proves it. Recorded because it nearly cost the result: the leg's first ordering checked the CONTROL first, so a SUCCESSFUL replay — which consumes the token and therefore breaks the control — reported itself as "this leg proves NOTHING". Read the replay first; a broken control corroborates it |

The port was then held by pid 59502 — `rexenv --dns-agent`, a DIFFERENT process from the app — `rex status` read `answering (agent, udp 15353)`, `dig @127.0.0.1 -p 15353 anything.rex` returned `127.0.0.1` and `https://mstest.rex` still served 200, so the handoff did not cost resolution on the way through. **What this run does NOT cover**: the race was induced rather than produced by a real login, so the timing is representative, not identical; the SMOKE leg keeps the login check **Two more legs, 3 Sep 2026 review**: (1) the handoff was spawned from the STARTUP door into in-process mode only — the watchdog's agent→in-process fallback entered the same state with no handoff, so a wedged-then-relaunched agent could never win the port back for the rest of the session; both doors spawn it now. (2) A rebind failure after the probe window was latched as `Down` — but "address in use" there almost always means the agent took the port just AFTER the last probe, i.e. the handoff succeeded late; the failure arm re-probes and reports `Agent` when it answers. (3) `drop(service)` only REQUESTED the abort — the socket lived until the scheduler dropped the task, so "release before kickstart" was true of the code order and not of the file descriptor; `DnsService::shutdown` awaits the aborted task. |

The catch is `env[MAIL_*]` instead, which wins because Laravel's `LoadEnvironmentVariables` builds an IMMUTABLE Dotenv repository — a variable already in the process environment is never overwritten by `.env`. Both halves travel as ONE value through ONE setter: a pool holding the shim but not the environment catches WordPress and delivers Laravel, which is precisely the bug. The `async` and moved-wiring plants are compiler-caught rather than test-caught, which is the stronger place for them |

| 328 | binaries.rs (`PIN_MARKER`, `cache_matches_pin`, `write_pin_marker`, `resolve`'s cache check) + examples/php_tools_check.rs | **A cached binary is the bytes we PIN, not merely a file that exists.** The cache is keyed `<name>-<version>` and `resolve` returned early on existence, so when a pin's BYTES changed at an unchanged version string, every machine holding the old artifact kept it **forever** — no re-download, no repair, no signal. Not hypothetical: rexenv's own 7.4.33 shipped without `phar` (WP-CLI and Composer are phars run through the site's PHP, so no WordPress action worked), was rebuilt at the same version, and the fixed artifact could not reach anyone who had installed the broken one. #90 records the same shape upstream — static-php.dev and FrankenPHP both rebuild release assets in place — where a checksum mismatch is the signal ON A FRESH DOWNLOAD and there is none for a warm cache. **The extracted binary cannot be hashed to detect this** (the pin is the ARCHIVE's digest, not the member's), so the digest verified at download time is recorded beside the result and compared as a string — no re-hashing, no cost on the warm path. A MISSING marker is stale only for self-hosted versions, deliberately: those are exactly the pins whose bytes can be re-issued at an unchanged version, and making absence stale for everything would re-download every binary on every existing install to catch a case upstream has not yet caused | ✅ `a_cache_dir_is_stale_when_its_recorded_digest_is_not_the_pin` (matching → cached; differing → stale; absent → asymmetric, asserted BOTH ways against real `php_self_hosted_tag` answers). ✅ **Proven against a genuinely stale cache 15 Aug 2026** — better evidence than a synthetic fixture: the machine held the broken phar-less 7.4, `php_tools_check` FAILED naming it, and after this change the same command dropped the dir, refetched build 4 and went to PASS with WP-CLI and Composer running on all seven minors |
| 331 | binaries.rs (`PREPARE_RECEIPT`, `PREPARE_REV`, `bundle_prepared_current`, `resolve_bundle`'s cache check, `is_cached`'s bundle arm) | **A published bundle tree is cached only while it carries a CURRENT prepare receipt** (S0.3, the bundle sibling of #328 and the neighbour of #86). `resolve_bundle` returned early on a member stat, and a stat cannot see "dyld refuses to load this tree" — which #319 published for real (an escaping `@loader_path` waved through over 49 Mach-Os, provider reporting success), making the broken tree unrecoverable in the field: never re-downloaded, never repaired. The receipt records the prepare-logic REVISION, stamped into staging after prepare+member-verify and before the atomic publish; fixing a prepare bug that could have published a broken tree = bump `PREPARE_REV`, which is what carries the fix to machines already holding the broken output. **Absence is stale — deliberately the OPPOSITE asymmetry from #328's pin marker**: every pre-receipt tree is from the era that includes the broken predicate, no stat distinguishes a good one from a poisoned one, and the cost is one refetch per cached bundle. `is_cached`'s bundle arm applies the same rule so the download planner cannot promise "cached" about a tree the resolve is about to drop | ✅ `a_bundle_tree_is_cached_only_with_a_current_prepare_receipt` (absent → stale, current → cached, past rev → stale; **plant-proven**: absence-reads-cached fails the first leg by name). The resolve-level wiring is the same three-line shape as `resolve`'s proven stale-pin path; its live half rides the next network-tier `mariadb_bundle_check`/`redis` resolve, which will refetch unreceipted trees once |
| 325 | binaries.rs (`PHP_7_4_33_*_SHA256`, `php_self_hosted_tag`, `PHP_VERSIONS`) + the `rexenv/runtimes` release `php_self_hosted_tag` names (spelled `php-7.4.33-1` here until the pin moved twice — read the function) | **rexenv ships a PHP it built itself, and that pin cannot drift — only 404.** #90 records the standing exposure: static-php.dev and FrankenPHP both REBUILD release assets in place, so a pinned URL's bytes can change under us and the checksum guard firing is the only signal. For 7.4 that exposure is GONE by construction rather than mitigated: the release is immutable, the tag is never reused, and a rebuild is `php-7.4.33-2` — so the worst case is a loud 404, never a silent swap. This is the first pin in the file with that property. **The pin was taken from the bytes rexenv itself will fetch**, not from a number CI printed: each artifact downloaded over the real `releases/download` URL, hashed locally, and cross-checked against the release's own SHA256SUMS | ✅ **run-proven end to end 14 Aug 2026**, twice over. By hand: the arm64 cli extracted and executed — `PHP 7.4.33 (cli)`, `mysqli=1 gd=1 intl=1`, and an `otool -L` closure of `/usr/lib` + `/System` ONLY, which is exactly what `relink_to_system_libs` accepts on a user's machine (a non-system dep there is a hard error at `resolve()`, far from this pin). Then through rexenv's OWN path: L1 `php_versions_check` (network) downloaded, checksum-verified, extracted, de-quarantined, relinked, ad-hoc signed and RAN both cli and fpm, asserting the reported version, mysqli and host arch (#324) — "all 7 PHP versions". **The resolvability assertion was FLIPPED in the same commit as the pin** (`a_self_hosted_php_never_points_at_static_php_dev`): it asserted 7.4 was unresolvable until the artifact existed, and asserts it resolves now. Those two facts must never disagree — a version that resolves without an artifact is a permanent 404 on a user's machine, and the test that "covers" the manifest only checks shape (#323) |
| 324 | examples/php_versions_check.rs + binaries.rs (the "bulk, not common, because mysqli" pin note) | **A pinned PHP build is the version it claims AND can serve WordPress — asserted, not printed.** The check resolved every `PHP_VERSIONS` entry, ran `php -v`, and tested only the EXIT STATUS while printing the version line for a human who was not reading. So a binary that ran fine but was the WRONG VERSION passed, and the check that looks like it proves "the 8.1 pin really is 8.1" proved "some php ran" — the assertion and the claim pointing at different things, this repo's recurring shape. It matters more now that PHP comes from more than one source (#323): a self-hosted artifact is one filename typo from being the wrong build, and **the checksum cannot catch that** — it proves the bytes are the ones we pinned, never that we pinned the right ones. Also closes a claim nothing checked: `core/binaries.rs` says it pins the "bulk" builds rather than "common" BECAUSE common lacks `mysqli`, and no layer tested for mysqli | ✅ L1 `php_versions_check` (network) — per version: `php -v` and `php-fpm -v` must REPORT the pinned version (cli and fpm asserted separately, since they are distinct artifacts with distinct checksums and one being right says nothing about the other), `php -m` must carry mysqli/curl/mbstring/openssl, and `file(1)` must report the host arch so a wrong-arch artifact is a loud failure instead of a slow Rosetta mystery. **Run green against all 6 real cached artifacts 14 Aug 2026** — the assertions are correct, not merely stricter. **Scope, stated:** still NETWORK tier, so `scripts/verify.sh` does not run it; a green verify says nothing about any of this |
| 323 | binaries.rs (`php_self_hosted_tag`, `php_url`, `php_sha256`'s empty-const rule, `manifest`'s PHP arms) | **A PHP version resolves to the source that actually PUBLISHES it, and a version with no artifact yet does not resolve at all.** `manifest`'s PHP arms gate ONLY on `php_sha256(..).is_some()` and hardcoded one static-php.dev template, so pinning a self-built version's four hashes first would have produced a manifest resolving to a **permanent 404** — with `manifest_pins_every_pinned_php_version` still GREEN, because it asserted the URL's SHAPE and every 404 in this family has the right shape. The failure would have surfaced only on a user's machine, as "php-fpm 7.4 download failed". So the branch lands BEFORE any checksum: `php_url` picks the source, and an EMPTY hash const reads as unpinned (the `php-debug` rule, now applied to a real version) so the version is wired but unresolvable until its artifact exists. The URL carries the **full immutable release tag**, not a stable base + version const — both existing upstreams (static-php.dev, FrankenPHP) rebuild assets in place, which this file documents twice; a rexenv-hosted pin can 404 but must never change bytes, and that only holds if a rebuild is a new tag | ✅ `a_self_hosted_php_never_points_at_static_php_dev` (asserts the pure URL builder directly, while the version is still unresolvable — it cannot wait for the pin, because by then the mistake has shipped; also asserts the tag is in the URL, that a static-php.dev-published version still comes from there, that all four `manifest` lookups are `None`, and that 7.4 is absent from `PHP_VERSIONS`) + `manifest_pins_every_pinned_php_version` strengthened from shape to HOST per version. **Plant-proven 14 Aug 2026**: deleting the branch fails the test naming the dl.static-php.dev URL it would have shipped |
| 320 | binaries.rs (`XdebugBottle`, `xdebug_bottle`, `xdebug_version_for`, `xdebug_spec`, `bundle_manifest`'s xdebug arm) | **Which Xdebug a PHP minor gets is that minor's ROW, never one app-wide pin.** Xdebug's support windows close: 3.1.6 is the last release for PHP 7.4 and no later one will ever exist, so a single `XDEBUG_VERSION` gate makes a frozen minor's bundle unresolvable **silently** — `bundle_manifest` returning `None` reads exactly like "this minor has no Xdebug", which is a real state (8.0), so the failure wears the disguise of a supported outcome. The four facts that must agree — version, formula, arm64 digest, amd64 digest — are ONE struct per minor so they cannot be edited apart: a version bumped without its hashes 404s, hashes bumped without their version resolve the previous cache dir. `XDEBUG_VERSION` survives as the DEFAULT the in-window minors reference, not as the law | ✅ `a_minors_xdebug_version_comes_from_its_own_row` (the spec follows a frozen row's formula/tap path/per-arch digests via the pure `xdebug_spec`, built OUTSIDE the table so the mechanism is proven before the first frozen minor ships — once 7.4 is in the table a broken gate is a live bug, not a testable one) + `bundle_manifest_pins_xdebug_per_supported_minor` (now asserts through `xdebug_version_for`, the same accessor the UI reads, so a frozen minor shows up instead of hiding behind the constant) + `xdebug_is_refused_for_php_80_and_unknown_minors`. **Plant-proven 14 Aug 2026**: with a frozen `"7.4" => 3.1.6` row added to the table, the OLD `v == XDEBUG_VERSION` gate returns `None` for `xdebug-7.4 @ 3.1.6` and the row gate returns `Some` — the plant was run in both directions and reverted |
| 319 | macos/mod.rs (`needs_tree_relink`, `resolve_loader_relative`, `relink_into_tree`'s verify loop) + examples/relink_tree_check.rs | **"In-tree" means the load command RESOLVES under the bundle root — a `@loader_path/` prefix is not itself proof of anything.** The predicate returned `false` for that whole prefix family, so a dep spelled `@loader_path/../../../../opt/<formula>/lib/…` — which is how EVERY non-system dep of the shivammathur `php@7.4` bottle is spelled — was skipped by the rewrite loop AND by the post-relink VERIFY loop whose entire job is "nothing unresolvable remains". `prepare_binary_tree` reported success over 49 Mach-Os and dyld then refused the binary (`Library not loaded: @loader_path/../../../../opt/tidy-html5/lib/libtidy.58.dylib`). **The guard's claimed surface was the whole set of load commands and its check was one syntactic prefix inside it** — the `guard covers claimed surface` family, and the fifth of that shape. Latent rather than live only because every bundle rexenv ships today comes from homebrew-core, which uses `@@HOMEBREW_PREFIX@@` and was correctly flagged (verified across the cached redis/mariadb/httpd/xdebug trees: five in-tree `@loader_path/../lib/…` entries, nothing else). Resolution is LEXICAL, not `canonicalize` — a dep may not be staged yet, and `Path::starts_with` is component-wise, so a sibling prefix (`…/php-7.4.33-evil`) cannot pass as containment. `@executable_path/` is deliberately NOT resolved and always rewritten: which executable loads a given dylib is chosen by whoever spawns it, so it is unanswerable from the tree | ✅ L0 `an_escaping_loader_path_is_not_mistaken_for_in_tree` (the three real bottle strings, the climb-past-root-and-back-down case, and `@executable_path`) + `needs_tree_relink_flags_only_unresolvable_deps` (in-tree paths from both a top-level and a nested Mach-O). ✅ L1 `relink_tree_check` (sandbox) — real Mach-O, real `install_name_tool`/`codesign`/`otool`: leg A refuses an escaping dep that isn't bundled, **leg B proves it is REPAIRED rather than merely refused** (rewritten to `@loader_path/../lib/…`), leg C is the control that an already-in-tree dep is left alone, leg D counts levels from the tree for a nested Mach-O. **Plant-proven at BOTH layers 14 Aug 2026**: restoring the old prefix test fails L0 by name and L1 leg A with "this is the shipped-dead-tree bug". **Scope, stated:** the sibling failure — that `resolve_bundle` caches a tree it never loaded, because its early return only stats the `member` file (#86's neighbour) — is NOT fixed here; it is why this bug was unrecoverable in the field and it stays open in `docs/TODO.md` |

## core/logs.rs / services.rs / php.rs / site_env.rs

| # | Anchor | Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 102 | core/logs.rs (`is_safe_key` + every key-taking fn) | **A log key is a file NAME inside the log dir, and nothing turns one into a path without asking.** The key arrives from the Logs tab and from `rex logs`, and the functions here read, TRUNCATE and copy the file it names — so without the gate `../../.ssh/id_rsa` is readable, `../../../etc/hosts` is truncatable, and `log_download` copies whatever the caller named into the user's Downloads folder | ✅ L0 `safe_key_rejects_traversal_and_non_logs` (the gate refuses `..`, separators, non-`.log`) **+ `every_key_taking_function_gates_before_it_joins` (2 Sep 2026, the owed call-site half)**: every function taking a caller's `key` must ask `is_safe_key`, the gate must come BEFORE the path is built (a check after the join validates a value something already used), and the count is pinned at three — a FOURTH function is exactly the case this exists for, since the shape gets copied from whichever sibling is nearest. **Plant-proven ×2**: a new ungated key-taking fn, and the gate moved below the join |
| 330 | php.rs (`pool_fate`, `POOL_MISS_LIMIT`, `Pool::misses`, `reap_dead`) | **A pool is reaped on probe evidence only after consecutive misses; an adopted master is judged by positive identification, never `kill -0`** (B29b — the July review's finding that the fpm pool reap was one-miss while the adopted-service reap had already grown both protections in B29). One failed port probe on a loaded box must not cost a serving pool; a spawned child's `try_wait` exit is positive evidence and reaps on sight; a recycled pid or a `php-fpm`-titled orphan worker set (port green, master gone) accrues misses and reaps at the limit — the "UI shows running, every site hangs" failure must not be immortal just because the port answers. The miss arithmetic is `service_manager::adopted_reap_decision` shared, not re-expressed — one definition of the contract | ✅ `pool_fate_takes_consecutive_misses_for_probes_and_one_exit_for_evidence` (full decision table incl. the serving-port/unidentified-master case) + `reap_dead_needs_two_misses_and_identifies_adopted_masters_positively` (wiring: real backdated child + real TCP listener; identified-serving master's count proven to RESET). **Plant-proven both ways**: `POOL_MISS_LIMIT = 1` fails the table's first line by name; replacing the identification with `true` (the old `kill -0` semantics) fails the recycled-pid leg |
| 103 | services.rs:414 + apache.rs (the `[R=404,L]` deny) + frankenphp.rs (`dotfile_guard`) | ⚠ Dotfile paths 404, never reach fastcgi/PHP (all three templates) | ✅ **all three backends live, 15 Aug 2026** — `dotfile_guard_check` runs the SAME five probes (.env 404 + secret never on the wire, .git/config 404, dot-dir .php 404 + marker never executes, root `.well-known` exempt) against real nginx+fpm, real httpd sharing the same fpm pool via mod_proxy_fcgi, and real FrankenPHP's embedded PHP — each behind its own control leg (PHP provably executes first, or every 404 is vacuous). **Plant-proven per template**: commenting Apache's RewriteRule deny fails ONLY the Apache legs — the .env comes back `200 OK` with the secret on the wire and the hidden .php executes, the exact incident — and emptying frankenphp's `dotfile_guard` fails only its legs the same way. The isolation is the point: three templates are three chances to regress independently, and the probes fail by backend name |
| 104 | services.rs:122 | `-t` gate runs against a candidate the live pool never reads | ✅ `fpm_candidate_check` (15 Aug 2026, sandbox tier, real php-fpm): the candidate carries a DIFFERENT port + marker so any path that read it binds visibly wrong; the real conf stays byte-identical through the candidate write and BOTH gate runs; the live pool serves through them; and a full stop+restart with the poison candidate still on disk binds the REAL conf's port with the poison port never bound. Controls both ways — the production candidate passes `-t`, a corrupted one FAILS it (a gate that cannot fail validates nothing). **Plant-proven**: pointing the candidate writer at the real conf path fails four legs by name, ending with `restarted pool never came back` — the literal bricked pool the claim promises impossible |
| 105 | services.rs:315 | nginx never 413s an upload PHP would accept | ✅ 2 lib tests |
| 106 | services.rs:769 | Long requests not killed at 300s | ✅ lib test |
| 107 | services.rs:621 | Foreign nginx on our port never signalled | ◐ decision ✅; live half 🔨 L3 |
| 108 | php.rs:197 | Default-deny whitelist: stored values can't smuggle directives | ✅ 3 lib tests |
| 109 | php.rs:75 | Unsupported minor can never grow a debug pool | ✅ lib test |
| 110 | php.rs:905 | Alive-in-grace never reaped; dead master always | ✅ lib test |
| 111 | site_env.rs:4 | Per-site env never touches shared pools; unescapables rejected | ✅ 4 lib tests |
| 327 | core/services.rs (the fpm pool config generator) + examples/php_fpm_serve.rs | **The pool config rexenv generates is accepted by EACH minor's own php-fpm — not just the newest one.** The generator was written against 8.x and emits one file per minor, so "php-fpm accepts it" was only ever proven for the default version. **PHP 7.4's php-fpm is a different program** and can reject a directive 8.x takes; the failure would land on a user's machine as a pool that will not start, on the one version most likely to be chosen for a legacy site nobody wants to touch. The check now takes the version as an ARGUMENT rather than reading `PHP_VERSION`, because the claim is per-minor and a check that can only ask about the newest cannot prove it | ✅ L1 `php_fpm_serve 7.4.33` (sandbox) run 15 Aug 2026 against the REAL 7.4 binary rexenv had already downloaded and signed: `php-fpm -t` → *"test is successful"*, master bound to the FIXTURE port, then reaped and the port freed. Run with the developer's own stack live (nginx + all six 8.x pools) and verified not to disturb it — fixture port 9792, `common::sandbox` paths, `Reaped` guard. **Extended 15 Aug 2026**: `php_pools_serve` (SERVICE tier) then ran with the stack STOPPED and brought up **all seven pools together** — `PHP-FPM 7.4 127.0.0.1:9774 LISTENING` beside 8.0–8.5 — then stopped them, with each port asserted freed and the process table checked for survivors afterwards (this example's own historical failure was leaking title-rewritten workers). So the pool 7.4 gets is real and coexists with the others. **Still owed and NOT claimed:** a 7.4 SITE answering an HTTPS request end to end — browser → Caddy → nginx → 9774 → WordPress. That needs a real site, which is the developer's data |
| 326 | core/sites.rs (`ensure_server_runs_php`, called from `create`, `set_web_server`, `set_php_version`) + binaries.rs (`FRANKENPHP_EMBEDDED_PHP`) | **A FrankenPHP site cannot be pinned to a PHP major FrankenPHP does not embed.** FrankenPHP is served by the PHP compiled INTO it, never by rexenv's pools, so `php_version` is a promise it cannot keep — and until 7.4 shipped, the mismatch was silent and survivable (an 8.1 site served by 8.5). At 7.4 it stops being skew and becomes a different language: the removals PHP 8.0 made are the reason a site is still on 7.4 at all, so "silently served by 8.5" means "silently broken, with the version picker still saying 7.4". The rule compares MAJORS from data (`FRANKENPHP_EMBEDDED_PHP`, previously only a sentence in a re-pin comment) rather than naming versions, so it stays true when either pin moves. **Scope, stated and deliberate:** the same-major mismatch is NOT refused — it predates this, and widening the guard would break FrankenPHP sites that work today; it is a `docs/TODO.md` row, not a silence | ✅ `frankenphp_refuses_a_php_major_it_cannot_run` — all THREE ways to form the pair (create with it, switch the SERVER of a 7.4 site, switch the PHP of a FrankenPHP site), each asserting the row is UNCHANGED after the refusal, plus a positive leg proving an 8.1→8.5 same-major switch still succeeds. The third door is the point: create and server-switch are the obvious two, and covering only those is the partial-surface shape this ledger already tracks four instances of |
| 322 | core/php.rs (`security_end`, `eol_since`, `eol_since_on`) + state/models.rs (`PhpVersionView::eol_since`) + lib/php.ts + Settings.tsx / NewSiteDialog.tsx / SiteDetail.tsx | **A runtime rexenv offers that gets no security fixes says so, where it is chosen — and the answer is COMPUTED, never remembered.** `grep -ri 'eol' src/` returned ZERO: PHP 8.0 died 26 Nov 2023 and 8.1 on 31 Dec 2025, and the picker gave both the same face as 8.4. The stored-flag design is what produced that, so the table holds **dates** (php.net's published schedule) and "is it dead" is a comparison against today — true on the day it becomes true, with nobody to remind. The table can only be extended, never flipped, and the one way it rots — a new minor entering `PHP_VERSIONS` without a row — fails the build. The frontend formats the date and decides nothing (`lib/php.ts`), the same separation #321 exists to enforce. Shown in all THREE places a version is chosen or lived with, because most sites on a dead runtime arrived by import or by outliving the version rather than by picking it; and for WordPress the note names the outdated-PHP notice WP will show, since the same sentence met first from us is information and met first from WordPress is a bug report | ✅ `every_shipped_minor_declares_its_support_end` (the anti-rot guard — without it the failure is silent AND in the safe-looking direction: `eol_since` → `None` → no warning → a dead runtime reads as supported) + `eol_is_derived_from_the_date_not_remembered` (before/on/after each of 8.0's and 8.1's end dates, an unshipped minor, and — the one that proves it is a comparison rather than per-version literals — ONE future date making every shipped minor EOL) + `the_version_list_carries_the_eol_date` (asserted equal to core for every seeded row, and asserted NOT uniformly null, so a stubbed field cannot pass). **Scope, stated:** L2 unproven — no wk-check renders the badge, the create-dialog note or the Environment note, so "the user actually sees it" rests on the L0 data being right and on review. `mock.ts` carries real dates for 8.0 and 8.1 so the dev routes show both |
| 321 | core/php.rs (`list_versions`) + state/models.rs (`PhpVersionView`) + SiteDetail.tsx (`XdebugCard`) + types/index.ts | **The client renders core's Xdebug rule; it never re-decides it.** `XdebugCard` disabled the toggle on a literal `minor === "8.0"` — a second copy of `binaries::xdebug_supported`, free to disagree the moment the pinned bottle table changed, which is the "guard covers claimed surface" shape that has bitten four times here. The flag now travels on the registry row, DERIVED per read (no column to migrate, and a pin change moves the UI on the next fetch). **It is a separate VIEW type on purpose**: hanging the field off `PhpVersion` would make it true when `core::php::list_versions` built the value and `false` when `store::list_php_versions` did — a recorded-vs-derived disagreement inside one struct, the family `docs/TESTING.md` §3.5 tracks. The persistence type has no such field, so the wrong answer is unrepresentable rather than unlikely. `xdebug_version` rides along because it is per-minor (#320) and a control that named one app-wide version would lie for a frozen minor | ✅ `the_version_list_carries_cores_xdebug_rule_rather_than_the_ui_guessing` — every seeded row's carried value is asserted EQUAL to `binaries::xdebug_supported`/`xdebug_version_for`, the two are asserted to travel together (support without a version, or the reverse, each render a control that lies), and the row set is asserted NOT uniformly true, so an accessor stubbed to a constant cannot pass. 8.0 is the live not-supported row. **Scope, stated:** that the rendered control is actually disabled is L2 — `XdebugCard` has no wk-check yet, and `mock.ts` now carries the 8.0 row with `xdebugSupported: false` so the dev route shows the case rather than a tidy all-true fixture |
| 318 | core/php.rs (`unshipped_minor`, `unshipped_patch`) + its consumers in core/php.rs, core/sites.rs, core/binaries.rs, mcp_server/scratch.rs, examples/mcp_scratch_check.rs | **The "a version rexenv does not ship" fixture is DERIVED from `PHP_VERSIONS`, so it cannot outlive the belief that made it true.** `7.4` was that fixture in eight asserts, one live example and two manual steps, chosen on a comment saying static-php.dev would never publish it — and the moment 7.4 gains a pin, `is_outdated_php_cache("php-7.4.33")` keeps **passing for the opposite reason**: 7.4.33 becomes the pinned patch, so "not outdated" is trivially true and the unpinned-minor branch it existed to cover goes untested. Same family as #224's derived refusal set and the same defect as the ledger's vacuous-green rows: the assert and the thing it claims to check stop pointing at each other, silently. Candidates are minors OLDER than any floor rexenv plans, and exhausting them PANICS rather than returning a shipped one — a fixture that cannot be found is a loud failure, never a quiet substitution | ✅ `the_unshipped_fixture_is_derived_from_the_pinned_set` (absent from `patch_for_minor` AND `available_minors`, and still port-derivable so the refusal/cache branches take their real paths). **Plant-proven 14 Aug 2026**: adding `7.2.34` to `PHP_VERSIONS` and re-running the four consumers stays green — the fixture moved to the next candidate on its own, which is exactly what the `7.4` literal could not do. **Scope, stated:** `docs/SMOKE-TEST.md` step 12 and `docs/PUBLISH-TESTING.md` step 5 are human steps and still carry a literal (7.2); both now say to check the shipped set first, which is prose, not a guard |
