The New-site dialog simply not offering the option is NOT the guard: `rex site create --db postgres` and the MCP `create_site` tool both parse the engine out of a caller-supplied string (`SiteDbEngine::parse_db`) and reach the same insert. (1) The ops were free functions in `core::database` called by name from eight places — honest while every site engine spoke the MySQL protocol and MariaDB was a second path to the same client. PostgreSQL has its own client, dump tool and DDL, so the choice moved onto `DbEngine` beside `start`/`data_dir`, the `database::` functions became `pub(crate)`, and the client carries its engine so the dispatch cannot be lied to (#329). (2) **`psql` exits 0 after a script whose every statement failed** unless `ON_ERROR_STOP=1` is set — the one failure in this feature that produces a WRONG ANSWER (an import that imported nothing, reported as success) rather than an error, so the flag is in the base argv of every invocation. Also PG-specific and easy to get wrong quietly: no `CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS` (idempotence is ours, two statements, not a swallowed 42P04 that would swallow every other create failure), `WITH (FORCE)` on drop (one open Adminer tab otherwise fails site deletion with a message the user cannot act on), and template databases excluded from `db_sizes` | ✅ L0 `psql_args_stop_on_the_first_error` (the whole array pinned — five invocations share it), `a_site_op_refuses_a_client_from_another_engine`, `redis_hosts_no_site_database`, `create_and_drop_reject_unsafe_names`, `drop_forces_other_sessions_off`, `the_connect_bound_is_an_env_var_both_libpq_tools_read`. ✅ **L1 live 9 Sep 2026, `examples/postgres_site_db_check` (network tier), all green against real PostgreSQL 18.6.0** — because whether a client agrees with a piece of SQL is not a question Rust can answer: create twice, a good dump imported, **a deliberately broken dump REFUSED** (`exit 3`, `relation … does not exist` — the ON_ERROR_STOP leg, which is the only place the flag's absence would ever have shown), an empty file refused before the spawn, `db_sizes` returning a real byte count with `template0` absent, `pg_dump` → re-import into a second database, `WITH (FORCE)` drop + a no-op re-drop, and a MySQL client refused by a PG op by name. **The port stayed a PARAMETER on every op** (rather than `self.port()`, which is what the plan said): the live examples run fixture servers on private ports 13396-13399 precisely so a check can never touch the owner's real databases, and folding the port in would have quietly pointed all of them at the production MySQL |

| 545 | core/php.rs (`pdo_pgsql_supported`, `oldest_pdo_pgsql_minor`) + core/sites.rs (`ensure_engine_supports`, the second refusal) + core/binaries.rs (`php_self_hosted_tag`) | **Whether PHP can reach PostgreSQL through PDO is a per-MINOR fact, recorded from measurement — and where it was false, rexenv fixed the runtime rather than the record.** Laravel's `pgsql` connection and the Blank-PHP starter's `db.php` both go through PDO, so this one fact about a bundled binary decides whether PostgreSQL sites can exist at all. Measured 9 Sep 2026 against real clusters (PostgreSQL 16.14 / 17.10 / 18.6) with the bundled static PHP 8.3 and 8.4: `pg_connect()` works, the bundled `psql` works, and **`new PDO("pgsql:…")` accepts the socket and never sends its startup packet** — the server closes it on `authentication_timeout` (60s on a stock cluster) as `SQLSTATE[08006] server closed the connection unexpectedly`, and `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` does not shorten it. The same call from a Homebrew PHP 8.2, same server, connects instantly, so it is the build and not PostgreSQL. The build agrees from the other side: `extension_loaded("pdo_pgsql")` is false on all seven versions while `PDO::getAvailableDrivers()` advertises `pgsql` on six of them — **a driver that claims to exist and then stalls for a minute is worse than no driver, which would have failed instantly and named itself**. So Laravel/Blank-PHP on PostgreSQL is REFUSED at `create_recording_ownership` while the constant is false: the alternative is a site that provisions cleanly, gets a database and a wired `.env`, and then hangs on its first query with an error naming neither PHP nor rexenv | ✅ **L1 `examples/laravel_postgres_check` (network tier), green 9 Sep 2026 — and it goes red on DISAGREEMENT with the record, not on the bug going away** (the `wp_dns_check` shape, #381): all seven bundled PHPs are asked whether `pdo_pgsql` is loaded and each must match the constant, then the default PHP is pointed at a real cluster — `pg_connect` as the control (proving the server is reachable, so the PDO result is about the driver) and PDO as the subject. While the constant is false it additionally asserts the FAILURE SHAPE — a stall the server ends, not a refusal the client raises — because that is the half that makes a developer blame PostgreSQL. The cluster runs with `authentication_timeout=5` so the measurement costs seconds. ✅ L0 `postgres_is_refused_at_the_insert_chokepoint_for_two_different_reasons` — the two refusals must not blur: WordPress's is permanent and about `wpdb`, this one lifts, and the test is written against the CONSTANT so flipping it opens the door instead of failing. **And the unblock happened, 10 Sep 2026.** `rexenv/runtimes` now BUILDS 8.1-8.5 (releases `php-8x-1` and `php-8x-2`, immutable) with `pdo_pgsql`, gated on a real PostgreSQL connection in CI — because `php -m` and `getAvailableDrivers()` had both reported support the upstream artifact did not have, so only a socket could tell — and on extension PARITY per minor against a module list generated from the artifact each one replaces. **8.0 stayed upstream's**: it passes every gate on arm64 and aborts on x86_64 inside static-php-cli's own sanity check (`php -n -r 'echo "hello"'` exits 6, no output), reproducibly across four runs with three different extension subsets excluded in turn; unexplained, EOL since Nov 2023, and half an architecture is not shippable. So the record became a FUNCTION of the minor, the refusal names the minor the site is on AND the oldest one that works (computed from `PHP_VERSIONS`, never typed — it is the sentence users act on), and the same live check now proves the driver works instead of proving the gap. Re-run 10 Sep 2026: 7.4/8.0 `absent`, 8.1-8.5 `loaded`, and on the default minor a full Laravel-shaped session — connect, DDL, prepared insert, read back — **then a REAL Laravel app**: `composer create-project`, the `.env` wired by `DbSettings::for_engine`, `artisan migrate --force`, and the `migrations` table found in the PostgreSQL database by asking the CLUSTER rather than trusting artisan's exit code. The sqlite leg of that is the one worth keeping: the skeleton's own post-create script migrates to `database/database.sqlite` BEFORE rexenv wires anything, so "the sqlite is empty" was a wrong assertion that failed a green feature — what holds is that the file does not GROW after wiring, which is what distinguishes our migrate from the skeleton's and would catch a `.env` left on sqlite (success in the log, nothing in the database) |
| 546 | core/laravel.rs (`DbSettings::for_engine`) | **Driver, port and superuser are one decision, taken from the engine.** They were typed at the provisioning call site (`connection: "mysql"`, `username: "root"`, `port: engine.port()`) — three literals beside one derived value, which is the shape that lets two of them stay right while the third goes wrong. A `pgsql` connection on 13306 reaches MySQL, whose handshake libpq cannot read, and the error names neither engine; `postgres` as a MySQL user is "access denied for user"; both read as rexenv being broken. Laravel has no `mariadb` driver before 11.x, so both MySQL-protocol engines are `mysql` and differ only in the port — which is exactly why the mapping deserves to be in one function rather than remembered | ✅ L0 `the_engine_decides_driver_port_and_superuser_together` — all three engines' triples pinned, plus the `.env` the settings produce: one answer per key and the skeleton's own `DB_CONNECTION=sqlite` gone, since a leftover would point migrations at a FILE and succeed while doing nothing to the database the site was given |

| 547 | commands/rewrite.rs (the driver refusal) + core/dbimport.rs (`Driver::for_engine`) | **The one-click config rewrite refuses a config whose DRIVER is not the one its database speaks, instead of rewriting host and port under it.** `RewriteKey` is a closed set — Host, Port, User — so a `DB_CONNECTION` change is unrepresentable by construction (D1). That is right, and it means the rewrite has to REFUSE the case rather than do half of it: a `.env` still saying `mysql` with its port moved to 15432 sends a MySQL client at PostgreSQL, where the handshake is unreadable to both sides and the error names neither — the same failure ledger #546 prevents one layer down by deriving driver, port and user together. Laravel's `Str::studly` calls `mb_split`, so **every `artisan` command on every Laravel site died** — `Call to undefined function Illuminate\Support\mb_split()` — on php-8x-1 and -2, whatever database the site used. | 423 | core/terminal.rs (`login_args`, the spawn) + examples/terminal_check.rs | **The built-in terminal spawns a LOGIN shell — otherwise it is a terminal that cannot run the developer's tools.** rexenv is launched by launchd, so its PATH is the bare `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, and a non-login zsh reads only `~/.zshrc`: never `/etc/zprofile` (path_helper → `/etc/paths`) nor `~/.zprofile` (`brew shellenv`). `code`, `rex`, brew git and every version-manager shim answered `command not found` inside rexenv while working in Terminal.app (which runs `login -pf`) — **reported from the app's own terminal 27 Aug 2026, not found by review**. Unknown shells get NO flag: a bad flag fails the spawn, and a short PATH beats a terminal that will not open. The bundled-PHP prepend still wins, because the injected `export` runs AFTER rc | ✅ `known_shells_are_launched_as_login_shells` (L0) + `terminal_check` (L1, sandbox tier). **The example's own PATH was the fixture that hid this**: it inherited a developer shell's PATH, under which a non-login shell passes every assertion. It now shrinks PATH to the launchd four BEFORE opening the session and requires the shell to echo back a PATH containing `/usr/local/bin` — a dir only path_helper, i.e. only a login shell, can restore. **Not covered:** shells outside the recognised list, and the startup COST — a real `~/.zshrc` takes seconds, which is why the deadlines are 30s and a slow rc will look like a slow terminal |
| 424 | components/terminal/SiteTerminal.tsx (`live`, `parkingBay`, `MAX_LIVE`) | **A terminal session outlives the tab that shows it — and the DOM node is moved, never rebuilt.** Reported 27 Aug 2026: leave the Terminal tab, come back, and the shell had been killed and the scrollback disposed, so a long `composer install` died on an alt-tab with its output gone. Sessions now live in a module map keyed by site. Two mechanics are load-bearing and neither is obvious: xterm cannot be `open()`ed twice, so the container is MOVED; and it is parked in an offscreen holder INSIDE the document rather than detached, because a detached node measures 0×0 and the renderer sizes itself from that. Bounded at 4 live shells — each is a full login shell (#423) — and an evicted one reopens on the next visit, which is what every session did before | 🔨 **L2** — a harness route can mount the tab, run a command, unmount, remount and assert the earlier output is still on screen and the PTY id unchanged. | 310 | repo.rs (`stash_push_args`, `git_stash_push`) | **A stash never takes IGNORED paths, and never reports a stash that isn't there.** `-u` is deliberate (untracked in, so the tree is genuinely clean and the checkout that prompted this can't still be blocked); `-a` is the flag that must never appear — under `.gitignore` here live `vendor/` and `node_modules/`, so `--all` would park minutes of install time in an entry that then conflicts with the next install, and the user's own build output would vanish from a button labelled "Stash". Second half: git's "No local changes to save" exits 0, so a clean tree is refused BEFORE running rather than passing as a stash the restore list can't show | ✅ `stash_takes_untracked_but_never_ignored_files` (argv, both directions) + L1 `repo_git_ops_check` legs 10/14 — an ignored `vendor/autoload.php` is asserted still on disk AFTER the stash (the `-a` regression passes every other assertion in that leg), and a clean tree refuses by name |
| 311 | repo.rs (`reset_hard_args`, `git_reset_hard`) + RepoPanel confirm | **Reset reverts tracked changes and DELETES NOTHING ELSE.** `git reset --hard HEAD`, no pathspec, and explicitly no `git clean`: a file the user wrote five minutes ago and never added survives an action whose name sounds like it takes everything — and it must, because unlike the tracked half there is no stash, no reflog and no remote to bring it back from. The list is read LIVE on every open and never cached in our own state: git renumbers on every pop, so a held `stash@{1}` silently names a different entry than the row the user is looking at, and a line that doesn't parse as three fields is DROPPED rather than guessed at | ✅ `a_stash_ref_is_exactly_stash_at_n_and_nothing_else` (8 refused shapes) + `the_stash_list_parses_messages_with_separators_in_them` (unit-separated, so a subject containing colons/spaces can't fake a field) + L1 `repo_git_ops_check` leg 15 — four revision expressions refused before git runs, and a stale index maps to "the list renumbers on every restore" rather than a raw git error |

The cost was never the posture, it was REPRODUCIBILITY: a bug in any wp-dependent feature was **unreproducible in a way nobody would guess**, because the difference is a directory neither party thinks to mention. **The fix that mattered was refusing to count.** This row named FOUR spawn sites; by the time it was worked there were SEVEN — `wp_step_streamed` and `commands/wp_install.rs` were added after it was written, by people with no reason to know a list existed. A guard asserting the four would have passed while two unpinned spawns ran a user's packages: a guard narrower than its own claim, the family that has cost this project four times in a month. So nothing counts spawn sites. There is ONE argv builder and ONE `Command::new(php_bin)`, and the tree is the coverage — a seventh site cannot be added around them without turning the build red. **Two decisions are load-bearing.** (a) The scope is *the commands rexenv runs for you*: `core::terminal`'s `wp` wrapper stays AMBIENT, because that is the user's own command line and pinning it would break `wp package install` from inside rexenv in a way that looks like our bug — and that exemption is what makes the tell's last sentence (*they still work in rexenv's terminal*) true rather than consoling. (b) The pinned value is a **FILE** beside the phar, not an empty directory: an empty dir is neutral today and populated by the first `wp package install` an agent sends through the raw runner, after which the pin is gone with every test still green. A file cannot contain `vendor/autoload.php`, so the pin holds by the shape of the path rather than by nobody writing there. Measured against the pinned phar 2.12.0: ordinary commands unaffected and silent, a package command answers `not a registered wp command`, `wp package list` refuses loudly with `mkdir(): File exists`. The tell itself is #261 | ✅ 6 lib guards + `wp_packages_check` (L1, sandbox tier). **All five L0 failure modes plant-proven**: a hidden argv in a file no list mentions (`every_wp_cli_argv_in_the_tree_is_pinned`, which names the file and the exemption), a second captured spawn, an argv built from the shared prefix with the pin dropped, and the two BEHAVIOUR guards without which deleting one line leaves the structure intact and the door unlocked — `the_one_captured_spawn_actually_carries_the_pin` (reads `Command::get_envs`, not the source) and `the_streamed_spawn_pins_the_env_it_was_handed`. The scan strips comments and carries `the_scan_reads_code_and_not_its_own_comments` in both directions, because the #235 copy guard shipped defective by reading its own explanation and **this one did too on first run** — it counted the two `Command::new(php_bin)` mentions in its own prose and failed at 3. **The L1 plants its own canary**: a fixture `vendor/autoload.php` registering `rexenv-canary` at `before_wp_load`, and leg A REQUIRES it to resolve when WP-CLI is pointed there — if it does not, the example reports **CONTROL LEG BROKEN, this run proves NOTHING** and exits, rather than reporting the pin as proven off a command that was missing for its own reasons (the vacuous shape one layer up; #236's lesson applied before it could bite). With that dir then EXPORTED into the process, legs B and C require `not a registered wp command` through both production spawns, D requires the terminal wrapper to still resolve it, E requires a freshly created pin to be a file. **Plant-proven five ways**, each failing alone: a canary without `before_wp_load` → broken control; the pin removed from `wp_command` → B resolved it; from `wp_step_streamed` → C resolved it; the wrapper pinned → D; `create_dir_all` instead of the file → E. **E's first version passed that last plant** — it read the pin file an earlier run had left beside the real phar, so it observed sticky state instead of exercising creation; it now does both. **SCOPE — what the green run showed, not what the claim hopes.** It showed that on this machine, under the real pinned phar, a package registering via `WP_CLI::add_command` does not resolve through either production path while the user's own `WP_CLI_PACKAGES_DIR` is exported. It shows nothing about a package that hooks WP-CLI some other way (a bootstrap step, `before_invoke`, a command replacing a core one), nothing about any machine but this one, and by design nothing about `core/terminal.rs`'s wrapper — which leg D pins the OPPOSITE way, since the tell's last sentence depends on it staying ambient |
| 229 | core/wp_packages.rs + build.rs (`embed_wp_dist_archive`) + resources/wp-dist-archive + scripts/build-wp-dist-archive.sh | **A command rexenv depends on is one rexenv CARRIES — the answer to #228 for our own dependencies.** `wp dist-archive` is not core WP-CLI, so the package tree is vendored, compiled into the binary (`include_bytes!` via build.rs codegen — no new crate, and it works identically for the app, the `rex` CLI and an example, which a `Contents/Resources/` path could not, since an example has no `AppHandle`), materialised under app-data version-stamped, and passed as an explicit `--require`. **What is pinned is checked against the artefact, not against a second copy of the number**: the version test reads `vendor/composer/installed.json` out of the EMBEDDED bytes, so a tree regenerated at another version fails the build rather than quietly changing what a user's zip contains. | 230 | core/dist_archive.rs (`argv`, `refuse_bad_target`, `resolve_for_compare`, `env_overrides`) | **The archive is never written where dist-archive would have put it, and the version that runs is the one we pinned.** `wp dist-archive <path>` with no target writes to `dirname(realpath(<path>))` — `wp-content/plugins/` for a cloned asset, and **inside the user's own repository** for a linked one. `argv` REFUSES such a target rather than callers being trusted not to pass one: the source itself, anything inside it, and **both** of a linked checkout's parents (the resolved `~/code/` AND the `wp-content/plugins/` the link sits in) — a checkout has two names and the rule has to hold under either, or it is true only under the one the reader didn't pick. Comparisons run on RESOLVED paths on both sides; the source is canonicalised and the target resolved through its deepest existing ancestor, because a lexical target leaves `/var/…` against a canonical `/private/var/…` and every comparison between them is silently false — on macOS `/var`, `/tmp` and `/etc` are all symlinks, so that is the ordinary case, not a corner. Measured 4 Aug: with a global v2.0.1 installed and our `--require` given, ours wins — but that is undocumented precedence between `--require` and package autoloading, the ⚠ shape, so the feature does not rest on it. | 231 | core/dist_archive.rs (`require_distignore`, `has_distignore`, `DISTIGNORE_STARTER`) | **No `.distignore`, no archive — a PRECONDITION, and the feature itself rather than a rail around it.** Without the file dist-archive archives everything (`.git`, `node_modules`) and reports `Success:` with exit 0, and there is no `.gitignore` fallback (pre-3.0, gone), so **the harmful outcome is the default one and it announces itself as a success**. Replaced with a **dangling symlink**, which discriminates: `Path::exists()` follows links and answers *false* for a name that is unmistakably taken, so `rename` destroys it while `hard_link` gets `EEXIST` and moves on. | 244 | state/models.rs (`Site::served_root`, `docroot_subdir`) + core/sites.rs (`LARAVEL_DOCROOT_SUBDIR`, the create rule) + state/db.rs v32 | **The folder we SERVE and the folder that IS the site are two facts, combined in exactly one place.** A Laravel project's front controller is `public/index.php` and its `.env` — the site's database credentials — sits one level ABOVE it, so serving the project root would put that file on a public URL. `path` keeps meaning the project root (what teardown removes, where composer/artisan run); `docroot_subdir` says what to append; `served_root()` is the only combiner, called by the nginx vhost, the FrankenPHP/Apache override and the desired-state map — three call sites that would otherwise each be an opportunity to get it right twice and wrong once. The subdir is recorded at create ONLY for a docroot rexenv OWNS: a LINKED project's stored path already points at the folder to serve (`docroot_rel` was applied at link time), so appending `public` there would serve a directory that does not exist. v32 backfills on that same rule, because Laravel rows created before the install flow hold the column default and would start serving a project root the day a real app landed in one | ✅ 2 lib tests — `served_root_is_the_subdir_and_never_the_project_root_that_holds_dot_env` and `v32_backfills_public_only_for_laravel_docroots_rexenv_owns` (all four ownership/type cases pinned, because being wrong in either direction is its own bug: too eager serves a missing folder, too shy publishes `.env`). **The wire-level half is inherited, not re-proven here**: that a request for `/.env` inside a served root 404s is #103's dotfile guard (nginx leg ✅ `dotfile_guard_check`) — this row is about `.env` never being inside the served root in the first place, which is the defence that does not depend on it |
| 245 | core/laravel.rs (`wire_env`, `set_env_key`) | **One answer per key in the generated `.env`, whatever shape the skeleton shipped.** `composer create-project laravel/laravel` writes `DB_CONNECTION=sqlite` live with the MySQL keys COMMENTED OUT (measured on laravel/framework ^13.8), and older skeletons ship them live with placeholder values. A writer that appends would leave `# DB_DATABASE=laravel` above `DB_DATABASE=wp_x_rex` — two answers, with dotenv readers disagreeing about which wins — so a commented entry is replaced IN PLACE and duplicates are dropped rather than left below. Deliberately NOT `core::confedit`'s refuse-rather-than-guess machinery: this is a file we generated seconds earlier, not a user's hand-edited config, and widening confedit's closed key set would have destroyed its own property (a password change is unrepresentable there, D1) | ✅ 4 lib tests. The fixture is the REAL generated file, copied verbatim from a live `create-project` run rather than invented — an invented one would not have had the commented block at all, and that block is the half that decides whether the app talks to this site's database or to a SQLite file nobody can see (the friendly-fixture family). A 5th test re-runs the writer over a genuinely generated `.env` when one is staged (`REXENV_LARAVEL_DOTENV`). **Amended 21 Aug 2026 — that last clause used to read "so the day the skeleton changes shape the hand-copied fixture cannot go stale in silence", and it can.** Nothing sets that variable: a repo-wide search finds the test and this row, and no example runs `composer create-project` (the clone-based checks read the repo's `.env.example`, which is a different file). So the 5th test skips on every run, silently — the guard against silence is itself silent. It is a MANUAL leg now and says so: `REXENV_LARAVEL_DOTENV=<path-to-a-real-generated-.env> cargo test --lib wire_env_over_a_real`. The stray SQLite file is deliberately left alone (it is the project's own file; deleting what Composer wrote would be a surprise) — it is simply no longer what `.env` names | 🔨 L1 (an example creating a Laravel site and asserting the site's MySQL database holds the migration tables — today the ordering is verified by the live run that FOUND it, 9 Aug 2026, and by nothing automated) |

Passing one for a Laravel or Blank-PHP site, or for a linked folder rexenv adopts as-is, used to be accepted, carried through the whole job, and do nothing — succeeding while doing nothing is the dishonest half. Refused BEFORE the row is inserted, so the caller gets a prepare-phase error with nothing created rather than a half-site; refused at the one choke point every create path funnels through (UI, CLI, MCP), so a caller that never sees a dialog is told the same thing | ✅ 2 lib tests, one of which pins the two functions against each other: the shapes the guard ADMITS are exactly the shapes `phase_defs` gives a blueprint phase to. | 429 | core/starter.rs (`seed`) | **Seeding is idempotent: a retry adds no rows and destroys none.** `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` + a seed guarded by `WHERE NOT EXISTS`, rather than DROP-and-recreate, which would delete whatever the developer had put in the table by the time they pressed Retry | ✅ `starter_seed_check` (L1 sandbox tier) — its own mysqld on a fixture port: seed → 4 rows, seed again → still 4, then a row the DEVELOPER inserted survives a third seed (5 rows, `mine=1`). The same run executes the PAGE'"'"'S own `SELECT` against the seeded table (L0 only proves the two files MENTION the same columns) and `php -l`s both generated files under the bundled PHP: a generated page with a syntax error cannot report itself, because PHP never gets far enough to render the error card |

## core/wp_dns.rs (loopback DNS for PHP — 10 Aug 2026)

| # | Anchor | Claim | Verdict |

| 251 | core/wp_dns.rs (module doc + `MU_PLUGIN`) | **A WordPress site can reach its OWN hostname from php-fpm.** The bundled static-php builds link libcurl against c-ares (`curl_version()['ares'] = 1.34.6`), which reads `/etc/resolv.conf` alone and never macOS split-DNS — so `gethostbyname("x.rex")` answered `127.0.0.1` while cURL to the same host returned errno 6, and WP-Cron (fire-and-forget, result never checked) stopped on every hosted site in total silence. The mu-plugin hands cURL the system resolver's own answer via `CURLOPT_RESOLVE` on `http_api_curl` | ✅ `wp_dns_check` — runs the REAL plugin source under the REAL bundled PHP against a fixture listener and asserts BOTH halves: unaided cURL fails with errno 6 (the bug still reproduces on this build) and the same request returns 200 with the plugin. A test that only proved the fix would go green the day the bug quietly changed shape |
| 252 | core/wp_dns.rs (`rexenv_dns_resolver_is_loopback`) | **Only zones served from THIS machine are redirected.** The plugin acts on a host only when `/etc/resolver/<tld>` exists AND names a loopback nameserver (`127.x` / `::1`); a company VPN's split-DNS (`nameserver 10.8.0.1`) and every public domain fall through untouched. Rerouting those to whatever the local resolver says would break a working setup in the name of fixing a local one — and the TLD is concatenated into a filesystem path, so it is `[a-z0-9-]{1,63}`-checked first | ✅ `wp_dns_check` — the predicate is a separate PHP function precisely so the NEGATIVE cases are provable without root: ours (v4 + v6) true; VPN, LAN and empty resolver files false; a public host resolves to `null` (no resolver file at all) |
| 253 | core/wp_dns.rs (`ensure_for_site`, `ensure_all`) + commands/site_provision.rs (settle-ok) + commands/sites.rs (rename) + lib.rs (launch) | **Every WordPress site rexenv hosts has the file, and it never goes stale.** Nothing per-site is baked in (no domain, no TLD list), so a domain change or a new TLD needs no rewrite — but the mu-plugin SWEEP removes it at rename, so the rename path re-installs immediately; otherwise Change domain would silently un-fix cron until the next launch. Installed at the one path every create takes; the launch pass covers sites that predate the fix or lost the file | ◐ the writer is proven at L0 (idempotence, tamper-restore, every-layout removal, no placeholder) and the whole-fleet pass is plain code over `sites::list`; that each of the four call sites actually runs is 🔨 L1 (an example creating a site and asserting the file exists in its docroot) |

The claims are bounded by what is actually known: an INSTALL toasts from the settled job — including `partial` as its own outcome, because three slugs with one bad is neither success nor failure — and the start of one says nothing at all; an UPDATE toasts only on the success path, where wp-cli's non-zero exit for any failed item means the count is safe to state | ✅ `wk-checks/wptoast.js` covers all five: Activate/Deactivate (once each, verb + name), Delete (nothing on opening the dialog, the toast only after confirming), Update (a count on success; on `?update=fail` wp-cli's own "Only updated 0 of 1" and never a cheerful count), and Install ok/partial/running — `partial` asserted to keep its own wording and wp-cli's summary rather than rounding to either side, and a RUNNING job asserted to say nothing. | 249 | core/wordpress.rs (`UpdateKind`, `UpdateTracker`, `UPDATE_STEPS`, `update_streamed`) + commands/wordpress.rs (`run_update_streamed`, `wp_plugin_update`/`wp_theme_update`/`wp_core_update`) + WordPressManager.tsx (`useUpdateStream`, `ProgressBar`) | **An update bar reports WP-CLI's own steps — it never runs on a clock, and never claims a byte percentage WP-CLI does not report.** Added 9 Aug 2026 for plugins, extended the same day to themes and core: each was one silent wp-cli call, so WooCommerce, Elementor or a core release gave tens of seconds of a dead button and then a vanished row. Every field is sourced: `done` counts items WP-CLI announced as settled (a FAILED item counts and is LABELLED `Failed` — the run moved on, but it did not succeed), `phase` is the upgrader's own line, and `fraction` = settled items + the current item's STEP POSITION in a fixed, WP-authored sequence. A line that matches nothing (a table row, a warning) is logged and moves nothing, so noise can never advance the bar. Rows WP-CLI has not reached read `Queued` rather than showing a bar at zero as if work were happening. The current item is the argv-order name, refined by the slug in a wp.org download URL only when it parses as a slug — a premium plugin downloading from its own host keeps the argv name instead of showing a filename, and core (whose archive is named for a VERSION) reads no slug at all. **ONE parser across three nouns, not three**: it is WP's single `WP_Upgrader` wearing a noun, so only the settled line differs per kind — three copies would have been three places for the bar to start lying | ✅ 9 lib tests over transcripts whose every phrase was checked against BOTH ends of the pipeline rather than remembered — WP core's upgrader string tables (`class-wp-upgrader.php`, `class-plugin-upgrader.php`, `class-theme-upgrader.php`, `class-language-pack-upgrader.php`) for the phrases, and the pinned wp-cli 2.12.0 phar for the `strip_tags` + `&#8230;`→`...` transform that decides what a line actually looks like, plus wp-cli's OWN lines (`Using cached file '%s'...`, `Updating to version %s (%s)...`, `Cleaning up files...`, `Success: WordPress updated successfully.`). **Reading the sources found a bug the tidy fixture had hidden**: WP runs a LANGUAGE-PACK upgrade inside a plugin/theme update, and its `Translation updated successfully.` was being counted as an item finishing — one phantom item banked per translation pass, the bar running ahead of the work and reaching 100% with plugins still updating. It is a STEP now, asserted as never-a-settle. **Two faults from the FIRST real use, both in the wiring the tests do not reach** (fixed same day): (a) the bar sat at 100% "Updated" for seconds before vanishing — `onSuccess: onDone` where the caller's arrow implicitly returned `qc.invalidateQueries()`, and react-query AWAITS a promise returned from that callback, so the mutation stayed pending for the whole slow wp.org re-check; the hook now discards the return so no caller can block the bar by accident. (b) the update badge and arrow came BACK for a few seconds after a successful update — invalidating a query refetches but does not erase, so the checked pass's stale row kept being merged over the fresh list; the updated names are now dropped from that cache, falling back to the fast list's own truth until the real answer lands. Both are the same lesson as the row's ◐: **the parser was the tested half and neither fault lived there.** **Scope, stated: the PARSER is proven, the WIRING is not** — that the emit reaches the panel and a bar advances on a real download is 🔨 L2/L3, unproven at any layer today (`docs/TODO.md`)   **L2 30 Aug 2026 — the WIRE is now proven, and it had never been exercised at any layer**: `wk-checks/wpupdate.js` (A) starts a real two-plugin run through `PluginsPanel`, emits `wp-update://plugins/dev` payloads the way the backend does, and reads the RENDERED bar — 0 → 25 → 60, with the phase word, the `n of m` counter and the plugin wp-cli is sitting on following the stream, and the bar CLEARING when the run ends. Plant-proven by severing the listener (`onWpUpdate(…, () => {})`): five assertions fail, the bar sits at 0 and the phase stays "Starting". **What unlocked it was the harness, not the claim**: `DevGitPanel` answered `plugin:event|listen` with a bare `1`, so every `listen()` in the app resolved and could never fire — no streamed UI had ever rendered under WebKit. `mockIPC(…, { shouldMockEvents: true })` turns that on for update progress, repo jobs, installs and provision alike. The row said this needed a real site for weeks; it needed a listener that could fire |

| 250 | WordPressManager.tsx (`verdict`, `isNewerVersion`, `settleAfterUpdate`, `useWpPlugins`/`useWpThemes`) | **A row may claim an update ONLY when the offered version is newer than the one on disk, and a finished update settles both caches before it refetches.** Added 9 Aug 2026, after the badge and the arrow survived a finished update by MINUTES on a real site — the second and worse half of #249's wiring faults, and the first fix (drop the checked row) was aimed at seconds when the fault was measured in minutes. Two independent causes, so two guards. TIMING: the checked pass on a loaded site takes tens of seconds to over a minute (every plugin against wp.org, plus every premium plugin's own API), so a check that started BEFORE the update is normally still in flight when the update ends, and its answer — describing the old disk — lands on top of the drop and restores the badge until the NEXT check finishes. `settleAfterUpdate` cancels that check FIRST, erases what BOTH caches say about the updated names (wp-cli exited 0: they are at the version it installed), then invalidates, so the refetch is the only writer left. TRUTH: `--skip-update-check` does not mean *no update info*, it means *read the update transient without refreshing it* — so both passes hand up claims that can be stale, and `verdict` is the ONE place either list decides one, refusing any claim whose target is not NEWER than the installed version. That makes "update to X" over an item already at X unrenderable whatever the source — in-flight check, stale transient, or a premium plugin's own updater caching its answer for hours. Compared segment-by-numeric-segment because a string compare calls `1.1.11` older than `1.1.3.8`; a non-numeric segment (`1.2.0-beta1`) can't be ordered, so any difference counts as newer — the conservative answer for an offer. Core is the same fault in one item: its update now invalidates `wp-info`, the source of the core version every other card shows | 🔨 L2 — the BACKEND half was measured before writing this (wp-cli 2.12.0 + WP 6.x on a real site: `plugin update` wipes the `update_plugins` transient, so the fast pass after an update is honest, and a checked pass after it is honest too — i.e. **no backend layer produces the stale claim; every one of them is a frontend cache/timing artefact**, which is why the fix is entirely in the panel). ✅ **the rule is held where it RENDERS since 13 Aug 2026** — `wpverdict.js`, over two fixture rows in `DevGitPanel`'s `plugins=list`, because the rule is a pure function and this repo has no JS test runner to hold one; adding a runner for two assertions costs more than proving them through the real component. **Both directions, each failing differently**: `stale-claim` (on 3.4.1, offered 3.4.1) must render NO badge, NO icon-button and NO `→` — the stale-source claim that made a finished update's badge come back; `numeric-order` (on 1.1.3.8, offered 1.1.11) MUST render all three, which is the direction a naive `next > have` breaks and a check testing only the first case would let through. **Plant-proven both ways**, the string-compare plant being the instructive one since it is the fix someone reaches for. A third row (`wordpress-seo`, the ordinary case) is asserted to still offer, so the two above cannot pass on a panel where nothing ever offers anything, and every row is proven PRESENT first — "no badge" is also what an empty list looks like. **Correction recorded**: the plan said `uireview.js` drives the panel over mock data; it does not — `DevGitPanel` + `wptoast.js` do, and the probe went there instead. The probe's own first version also reported the WORKING rows as broken, because the Update control is icon-only (`ArrowUpCircle`) and its version lives in the `title`, so matching on button text found nothing and read as "no offer" everywhere. The TIMING half (cancel-then-settle beats an in-flight check) still needs a real site — same run as #249   **L2 30 Aug 2026 — held, and the row's own attribution was wrong**: `wk-checks/wpupdate.js` (B) arms a late, STALE plugin-list response (`available` with an EMPTY target — the shape `verdict` lets through by design, since it cannot be ordered), puts it in flight, finishes a real update on that row, and asserts the badge does not come back; a neighbouring row that should still offer 22.4 is the anti-vacuity guard. **The first version of this check passed a plant and therefore proved nothing.** Measuring the four combinations showed `settleAfterUpdate` is REDUNDANT BY TWO — cancel-only keeps the badge off, invalidate-only keeps it off (react-query drops a resolution superseded by a newer fetch), and only removing BOTH brings it back. So the guard is the pair, not `cancelQueries` alone, and the redundancy is load-bearing in one direction: the invalidate refetches only while something observes the query, so the cancel is what holds when nothing does. The check holds the composite, user-visible claim and says in its own comments that it cannot attribute further **A PARTIAL update left the rows that did update stale** (post-0.4.0 review, 3 Sep 2026): `settleAfterUpdate` ran only in `onSuccess`, and wp-cli exits non-zero when ANY item fails — so "Only updated 2 of 3 plugins" landed in `onError` and the two that succeeded kept their old version and their badge until a manual Refresh. `onSettled` now invalidates the list — a re-read, never an optimistic write. |

## MCP server (PROPOSED — pre-implementation posture, `docs/archive/PLAN-mcp-server.md`)

Forward-recorded so nothing downstream describes MCP as sandboxed before the code
exists. Re-anchors to the toggle/registry code when it lands (M2).

| # | Anchor | Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 197 | PLAN-mcp-server.md §3.1 (D7) + §6.0 (the guarantee, re-read 3 Aug 2026) + mcp_server.rs (the copy guard's must-say list) | ⚠ The MCP server is NOT a sandbox: once a client has scratch code execution — or, from MCP parity (3 Sep 2026, #471–#474), a scope grant on one of the USER's own sites — it holds user-level power over rexenv and the machine (plugin activation is arbitrary user PHP; user PHP reaches the CLI socket), so the tiers and the grants deliver a paved road + no silent amplifier, NEVER containment. A grant bounds WHICH site and WHICH verb, never what code runs | 🚫 inherently unprovable — a *containment* claim would be FALSE, not merely unproven; the posture is the claim (accepted, D7). **Re-anchored to the toggle/registry code at M2, and the §6.0 guarantee re-read against the shipped tool list on 3 Aug 2026**: three sentences had drifted, all of them the §3.1(c) shape — *"your sites … are not reachable from any tool"* (true of MUTATION; M1's read tools reach every site and `tail_log` reads the user's own debug log, so the available reading, "an agent sees only what it creates", was never true of any shipped version), *"share it"* as a promotion trigger (removed by #214), and *"nothing the agent runs can write back to your checkout"* (true of the CLONE, false of `wp eval` running as the user). ✅ **None reached a user**: the card was rewritten honestly at task 8b *because the copy guard fired*, and the guarded text stayed true while the unguarded draft rotted — which is the finding. The visibility claim is now on the must-say list (`look at your sites`), **plant-proven**: trimming that clause from the card fails the build naming what the user would stop being told |

## core/app_update.rs (in-app self-update — the trust boundary, `docs/archive/PLAN-self-update.md`)

T1 of the plan. The DESCRIPTOR half is here; the swap, the relaunch and the surfaces
land in T3/T4/T6 and re-anchor these rows to the code that performs them. #517 is
forward-recorded as a posture because it is a fact about macOS, and it has now been
measured once (§T0) rather than read.

| # | Anchor | Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 517 | docs/archive/PLAN-self-update.md §T0 + scripts/probes/app-swap-probe.sh | ⚠ **macOS App Management does not protect an ad-hoc-signed bundle at all.** Apple documents the rule only for apps sharing a Team ID, and rexenv has none — so nothing was known about an ad-hoc bundle replacing ITSELF until it was measured. On macOS 26.6.2 the probe renamed its own bundle aside and back, swapped it atomically with `renamex_np(RENAME_SWAP)`, AND opened its own running executable for writing, all unrefused; the relaunched copy carried no quarantine and raised no dialog. That is what makes the swap possible, and it also means nothing stops anything ELSE replacing rexenv.app — one more thing Developer ID signing and notarization would buy (`docs/SIGNING.md`). Every update changes the ad-hoc cdhash, so TCC grants keyed to it are re-asked | 🚫 posture — a fact about an undocumented macOS behaviour, so it can go false silently on any release. **Measured 6 Sep 2026** by `app_swap_probe` (tier `demo`, both a clean and a quarantined leg; the quarantined one translocated, which is the state the pre-flight refuses). Re-measured per macOS major and on any release that touches the swap — SMOKE-TEST |
| 518 | core/app_update.rs (module doc, `verify_with`, `cached`) + core/updates.rs (`verify_signed_bytes`) | **The app installs only bytes whose SHA-256 an ed25519-signed document names, verified against the key compiled into THIS build over the exact bytes, and re-verified on every read.** TLS is transport privacy, never trust: the digest gate compares bytes to whoever supplied the digest, so an attacker-chosen URL with an attacker-chosen hash matches perfectly. The signature and the document are stored TOGETHER and re-checked on each read, because `rexenv.db` is `-rw-r--r--` and a stored "verified" flag would put the verdict where the attacker is. **One seam, not two**: both documents verify through `updates::verify_signed_bytes`, since a second copy of a signature check is a second place for a `map_err` to swallow a failure | ✅ L0 `both_manifest_modules_verify_through_one_seam` (plants a flipped byte and requires BOTH documents to refuse it, plus a foreign key against each) + `a_tampered_app_descriptor_cache_is_refused_on_every_read_not_trusted_from_a_flag` + `a_descriptor_bigger_than_a_descriptor_is_refused_before_it_is_parsed`. **Not covered:** the live fetch against the published document, which is T2's network-tier example and does not exist yet |
| 519 | core/app_update.rs (`accept_with`, `SERIAL_KEY`) + core/settings_access.rs | **A descriptor whose serial is not greater than the highest ever accepted is refused BEFORE any write, and the high-water mark is its own key, Denied to `rex config`.** Equal is not stale — it is the ordinary state on every launch after the first, so it is accepted and writes nothing; a replay is an OLDER document served in place of a newer one, which is how a host holds a machine on a superseded build, and the signature alone does not stop it. The app's mark is a DIFFERENT key from the PHP manifest's: one document moving must never move the other's floor | ✅ L0 `the_app_descriptor_serial_is_its_own_high_water_mark` (asserts the older document is refused AND that the stored document is still the newer one, i.e. the refusal precedes the write; asserts the PHP mark is untouched) + `every_key_this_module_owns_is_ruled_on_and_the_signed_chain_is_denied` |
| 520 | core/app_update.rs (`offer_for`, `NoOffer`, `well_formed_version`) | **An offer is a LIVE comparison, never a stored flag** — strictly newer by numeric segment, three all-digit segments (so a prerelease is not representable, rather than filtered by a spelling somebody guessed), `minAppVersion` satisfied, `minimumSystemVersion` ≤ this Mac, and not the skipped version. The skip is a VERSION STRING compared to the offer, because a "skipped" boolean answers one question forever and would hide every later release. A macOS version that cannot be read fails CLOSED: no offer, since an update that will not launch is worse than no update | ✅ L0 `an_app_release_is_offered_only_when_strictly_newer_than_this_build` (incl. 0.10.0 vs 0.9.0, which a lexical compare gets backwards), `a_prerelease_is_never_offered_to_a_release_build` (six spellings), `a_skipped_version_is_compared_live_against_the_offer_not_stored_as_a_flag` (skipping 0.6.0 must not hide 0.6.1), `a_release_needing_a_newer_macos_is_not_offered_and_an_unknown_host_fails_closed`, `a_release_that_needs_a_newer_rexenv_to_install_is_not_offered`. **Not covered:** that the UI renders the refusal reasons — T6 (L2) |
| 521 | core/app_update.rs (`ALLOWED_RELEASE_PREFIXES`, `structural_check`) | **The artifact URL must sit under a compiled-in `releases/download/` PREFIX — a path, not a host — and the document is trusted for its signature, never for its location.** `https://github.com/` alone would let any account serve the bytes the app replaces itself with, and anyone can create an account. Checked on the URL as WRITTEN: GitHub's final hop is a signed CDN URL that expires in about an hour, so a check after the redirect would be checking the wrong thing. The digest and size are structurally bounded too (lowercase 64-hex; non-zero and ≤ 200 MiB), so a signed document naming a 4 GB "update" is refused before a byte is streamed | ✅ L0 `the_artifact_url_must_sit_under_a_release_download_prefix_not_a_bare_host` (four rejected URLs incl. a foreign account and a plain-http one, plus an assertion that every prefix IS a path under `github.com/rexenv/`) + `a_malformed_digest_or_size_is_refused_rather_than_downloaded` |
| 522 | core/macho.rs (`archs`) | **"Is this build universal" is read from the file's own Mach-O headers, never from `lipo`.** The swap must refuse a thin staged bundle — shipping one turns an update into a machine that cannot launch its own app — and that check runs in `core`, which may never name an OS or shell out (#163). `None` means the file tells us nothing (not a Mach-O, truncated, a 64-bit fat header) and a caller must never read it as a pass | ✅ L0 `archs_reads_a_fat_header_and_a_thin_one_and_refuses_everything_else` over synthetic headers, incl. a fat header of architectures rexenv does not ship and one claiming 9000 slices. **Not covered:** the check being wired into the swap — T3 |
| 523 | core/app_update.rs (`CheckCache`, `store_check`, `state`) + commands/app_update.rs + Settings.tsx (`AppUpdateCard`) | **"Checked N ago" rests on ONE value holding the timestamp and what the check found, written ONLY after a check succeeds.** Two keys would let a crash between the writes leave "checked just now" over yesterday's answer — the dishonesty the line exists to prevent, and the rule `php_upstream` already paid for. A failed check writes nothing and the card says the check failed rather than aging it into a success; before any check has ever run the card says THAT, which is a third sentence and not "up to date" | ✅ L0 `the_check_cache_is_one_value_written_only_on_success` + L1 `app_update_check` (network; the live accept path writes it). **Not covered:** the rendered card — T6 (L2) |
| 524 | lib.rs (`check_for_app_update`, `spawn_app_update_poller`) + core/app_update.rs (`auto_check_enabled`) | **Automatic checking is honoured BEFORE any I/O, and "Check now" always works.** The setting is read before the request is made, not applied to the answer afterwards, and the 6 h poller re-reads it every tick so turning it off stops the NEXT request rather than only the display. Junk reads as ON — the opposite of `start_services_on_launch` and deliberately: there the safe direction is that nothing starts, here it is that a user still hears about a security release. The request carries `rexenv/<version>` and nothing else, and the whole path is best-effort: no key, no network, a stale serial or a bad signature are log lines that leave the app exactly as it was | ✅ L0 `auto_check_is_read_before_the_request_not_applied_to_the_answer` (source scan over the launch sweep and the poller) + `the_auto_check_setting_is_on_unless_it_says_exactly_false`. **Not covered:** that six hours actually elapses — nothing below L3 can watch a clock that long, and the loop shape is the same one four other tasks in `lib.rs` use |

| 525 | platform/macos/app_bundle.rs (`swap`, module doc) + platform/traits.rs (`AppBundle`) | **The bundle is replaced only by a whole-bundle rename of a VERIFIED sibling — never a write inside the launched bundle, never a copy-over.** One `renamex_np(RENAME_SWAP)`, so no instant exists where the install path holds nothing (the state a KeepAlive LaunchAgent would respawn into); the fallback is rename-aside + rename-in with a restore, for a filesystem without the atomic exchange. Staged as a sibling in the bundle's own parent, which makes `EXDEV` impossible by CONSTRUCTION rather than by a check — the plugin stages in `$TMPDIR` and that is upstream issue #3505's data loss. Not a rule about what macOS permits (T0 measured that it permits everything here) but about what is safe: overwriting a running Mach-O in place invalidates pages the kernel is executing, and a copy-over leaves a hybrid of two builds | ✅ L1 sandbox `app_bundle_swap_check` — stages, swaps and reports `AtomicSwap` on a fixture bundle in a fixture Applications folder, then asserts the install path holds the new version and the receipt names where the old one went. **Not covered:** the swap against the REAL /Applications bundle — T0's probe measured that once, and SMOKE re-measures per macOS major |
| 526 | platform/macos/app_bundle.rs (`stage`, `verify_staged`) | **A staged bundle becomes the installed app only after it is what the signed release promised**: `CFBundleShortVersionString` equal to the offered version, the identifier, the executable name the relaunch resolves through, every required binary present (`rex` included — a bundle without it silently breaks every open terminal), each of them carrying every architecture this build ships, and a clean `codesign --verify --deep --strict`. **Every failure path removes the staging directory and leaves the installed bundle byte-identical**, and each names what was actually wrong rather than "verification failed" | ✅ L1 sandbox `app_bundle_swap_check` — a wrong version, a missing sidecar and a THIN bundle are each refused (the thin fixture is built with `lipo -thin` so the leg always runs), the installed bundle is compared byte for byte afterwards, no staging directory is left behind, and an anti-vacuity leg stages the same thin bundle when only its own architecture is required |

The decision is a pure function over facts the platform gathered, so every branch is provable without a Mac. | 537 | scripts/release-assets.sh + scripts/check-versions.sh + scripts/check-app-manifest.sh + .github/workflows/release.yml | **A release's update archive is checked as the thing a user RECEIVES, not as the thing that was built.** §A0 has always inspected the `.app` `tauri build` produced; a self-updating user gets whatever comes back out of `rexenv_<V>_universal.app.tar.gz`, so the script re-runs the per-slice checks — both binaries universal, `Dist_Archive_Command` AND the update public key in each slice, `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, Info.plist version — on the EXTRACTED bundle, and asserts the archive's layout (exactly one top-level `rexenv.app/`, no AppleDouble members; the extractor strips exactly one component and both other shapes produce a broken install). No asset may end in `_universal.dmg` but the dmg, because the tap selects the cask's asset by that suffix. The four-manifest version guard moved out of CI and into a script both entry points run — it had lived only in a workflow that does not execute while the repo is private, so five releases shipped with nothing checking it | ✅ scripted and RUN: `release-assets.sh` is green against the real 0.5.0 build (6 Sep 2026), its layout guard is **plant-proven** (a `./`-prefixed archive is refused), `check-versions.sh` is **plant-proven** (a bumped `cli/Cargo.toml` is named), and `check-app-manifest.sh`'s ed25519 verification was proven against a locally generated keypair before it had a document to read — sign, verify, then tamper and be refused. **Not covered:** the first full `pnpm release:mac`, which is a human gate on the release that carries the first descriptor — PUBLISH-TESTING §A0-b and §M |

| 538 | src-tauri/src/core/updates.rs (`RELEASE_PUBKEY`) + `rexenv/runtimes` `scripts/publish-manifest.sh` + `scripts/publish-app-manifest.sh` (`EXPECTED_PUBKEY`) + `rexenv/homebrew-tap` `Casks/rexenv.rb` (`auto_updates true`) | **ONE ed25519 key signs PHP, Adminer and the app itself, and both publishers refuse to sign with a key the shipped app does not pin.** The honest cost of one key is that a compromise takes all three at once; what makes it survivable is that the public half is COMPILED IN, so rotation is an app release and old documents stop verifying the moment users update. The refusal exists because a wrong key signs perfectly well: it publishes a document every install rejects **in silence** — no error, no log, users simply stop being offered anything, which is indistinguishable from a forgery seen from outside. `rexenv/runtimes/docs/MANIFEST.md` §4 asserted this refusal before it existed (added 7 Sep 2026, the doc-asserts-a-nonexistent-guard shape this project has paid for twice). The cask declares `auto_updates true` so `brew upgrade` skips rexenv and the two updaters cannot install over each other | 🚫 **posture, and unprovable from this repo by construction** — the guard lives in two OTHER repositories, so no test here can run it. What this repo can prove it does: `scripts/check-app-manifest.sh` verifies the PUBLISHED document against this tree’s `RELEASE_PUBKEY`, which catches a rotation done backwards after the fact. Both publishers’ refusals were exercised live on 7 Sep 2026 with a throwaway key (refused before any download); the tap’s `brew style` is clean. The remaining gap is that nothing in CI here fails when the other repos drift — T11 is when the whole chain runs once for real |

| 539 | core/app_update.rs (`installed_pending`, `state`) + src/components/settings/AppUpdateCard.tsx (the `installed` phase) | **An update whose quit the user CANCELLED is its own state, and it outranks the offer.** Pressing Install with a tunnel up and choosing "Keep sharing" at the quit gate leaves the new bundle in `/Applications` with this process still the old build. The card read that as "an update is available" and showed the button again — inviting a second download of bytes already on disk, and never mentioning the restart that was the only thing left. The plan specified this state (§8, *"…takes effect when rexenv next opens"*) and it was simply not built; the apply’s mutation has no `onSuccess` because "the window goes away as part of succeeding", which is true of every path except the one a person can choose. Now read from the NOTICE ROW the swap already writes, so it survives the window closing, suppresses the offer, and clears the tray snapshot too | ✅ L0 `an_installed_update_whose_quit_was_cancelled_outranks_the_offer` (a notice naming the RUNNING version is a completed update, not a pending one; a different version suppresses `offered`, names the restart in `no_offer_reason`, and empties `current_offer`; junk degrades to "nothing pending") + ✅ L2 `uireview.js` `appupdate-installed-pending` — **plant-proven** 7 Sep 2026 by restoring the shipped behaviour, which fails both legs by name (`the card never says "keep rexenv running"`, `data-phase is "none"`). **Found by §M on a real Mac**, by a person doing the one thing the automated tiers cannot: answering a dialog |

| 540 | core/updates.rs (`bounded`, `PAIR_DEADLINE`, `fetch_signed_pair`) | **A check a person is watching always ends.** Both signed documents — the PHP/Adminer manifest and the app descriptor — are fetched through one seam, and that seam rested on reqwest’s `Client::timeout`. It is not a guarantee: a wedged connect or a resolver that never answers can sit inside `send()` past it. `binaries.rs` learned this as B34 and wrapped every attempt in `send_bounded`; this seam was written later and did not inherit the lesson. The deadline is now enforced above the HTTP stack, and it is TOTAL for the pair rather than per-request, because two 15-second waits is not a different answer from "forever" to someone holding a mouse | ✅ L0 `a_fetch_that_never_answers_ends_anyway_and_says_so` — hands `bounded` a future that never completes, which is the case no reachable host can reproduce and the only one that mattered; asserts the deadline is what ended it, that the message names the wait rather than reading as a network error, and that a future which DOES finish passes through untouched. **Plant-proven** 7 Sep 2026 by ignoring the deadline: the test then hangs past 45s, exactly as the app did. **And a failed check reads like a sentence.** What a user saw was `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/…/app-manifest.json: error sending request for url (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/…/app-manifest.json)` — the URL twice and reqwest’s internals once, for "no internet". The URL is compiled in: the reader cannot change it, and showing it makes a network problem look like a bug in rexenv. `FetchFailure` now carries the three shapes a reader can act on (unreachable / a status / unreadable), the URL and the library’s own words go to the LOG, and the deadline expiring reads the same as a failed connect because to a user they are the same event. ✅ L0 `a_failed_check_reads_like_a_sentence_and_not_like_a_stack_trace` — no message may contain `http`, `://`, `raw.githubusercontent`, `reqwest` or `error sending`; each must end in a full stop; and 404 must name the STATE ("nothing published yet") without the number, because that is the ordinary pre-first-release condition rather than a fault. **And the two callers no longer share a deadline** — `INTERACTIVE_DEADLINE` 6s for the button, `BACKGROUND_DEADLINE` 12s for the poller and the launch sweep, passed IN by each call site. That distinction is the whole of this row: the original comment said "nobody is waiting on this", which was true of the only caller that existed when it was written. ✅ L0 `the_interactive_check_and_the_poller_do_not_share_a_deadline` — a source scan, because nothing in a type can hold which constant a call site passes, and a "simplification" back to one would silently give the button the long wait again; **plant-proven** by pointing the command at the background constant. **Found by §M**, offline leg: the spinner ran past 40 seconds under a 15-second client timeout that never fired |
| 541 | macos/mod.rs (`app_bundle_path`, `TERMINALS`/`TermLaunch`, `open_in_terminal`) + commands/terminal.rs (`terminal_open_external`) | **A chosen terminal is handed a DIRECTORY or nothing — and the directory is named on the BACKEND side.** `open -a Terminal <file>` does not display the file, it RUNS it as a script, so this is the browser's http(s)-only guard with a sharper edge: the check runs before the terminal id is looked up, so it holds for an app that is not installed too, and the cwd itself is resolved from the site id (+ plugin/theme slug) through the same `core::terminal::asset_cwd` the built-in tab uses — the frontend never names a path. **The detection half is where this actually broke first**: the `/Applications` + `~/Applications` search inherited from the editor/browser tables finds no terminal at all on a stock Mac, because since macOS 11 Terminal.app lives on the sealed system volume (`/System/Applications/Utilities`) | ✅ L0 `open_in_terminal_refuses_anything_that_is_not_a_directory` — a real temp FILE against a real installed terminal (so it proves the guard, not a missing app), a path that does not exist, and an unknown terminal id with a file path (the ordering leg: the refusal must not depend on the lookup); a directory with that same unknown id must fail on the ID instead, so the guard is not passing everything. ✅ L0 `detect_terminals_finds_the_stock_terminal_app` — every Mac has Terminal.app and no user can move it, so a machine that detects zero terminals is the search being wrong, which is exactly what it was. **Measured live 7 Sep 2026** for the `Folder` shape on both terminals this Mac has: `open -a Terminal <site dir>` and `open -a iTerm <site dir>`, then the spawned shells' `cwd` read back through `lsof` — both sat in the site folder, not in `$HOME`. 🚫 The flag-driven rows (Ghostty, WezTerm, kitty, Alacritty) and Warp rest on each app's documented cwd flag and are UNMEASURED — nobody has them installed here. That is why a row is only added for an app whose cwd handling is documented, and why the flags go through `-n`: the failure mode of a wrong row is a window in `$HOME` under a control that named a folder |

The tally is recomputed, never hand-maintained (the hand-kept version drifted within
one day of being written):

```sh
for v in ✅ ◐ 🔨 🚫; do printf "%s " "$v"; grep -c "| $v" docs/CLAIM-LEDGER.md; done
```
